## Snapshot
Oracle is now a full-stack enterprise tech platform spanning (1) cloud infrastructure — OCI, including the GPU/AI "AI infrastructure" buildout; (2) cloud applications — Fusion ERP/SCM/HCM/CX, NetSuite, plus vertical/industry suites; and (3) the Oracle Database franchise, increasingly sold cross-cloud ("multicloud" on Azure/Google/AWS). The story has shifted decisively from a seasonal license business to a hyperscaler-scale, capital-intensive AI infrastructure provider riding committed customer demand. FY26 total revenue surpassed $67B (+16% non-GAAP op income), but the equity is now valued — and debated — almost entirely on the OCI/AI backlog ramp. Oracle co-CEOs Clay Magouyrk (infrastructure) and Mike Sicilia (apps/database) run the business; Larry Ellison remains Chairman/CTO; Hilary Maxson took over as CFO ~June 2026.

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) OCI cloud/AI infrastructure (GPU compute) · 2) Oracle Database (incl. cross-cloud "multicloud") · 3) Cloud Applications — Fusion ERP/SCM/HCM/CX + NetSuite |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Hyperscaler-scale AI customers (OpenAI dominant — Barclays models ~76% of OCI '28 AI capacity; $300B/5yr) + enterprises for apps/DB; 4 customers each >$8B in Q4 FY26 |
| **Key suppliers** | NVIDIA GPUs; datacenter build/lease partners (Crusoe, Vantage); power-as-a-service (VoltaGrid/Jenbacher); memory/SSD/HDD components (costs spiking) |

**✅⚠️ SCORECARD — RESOLVING THE STANDING 2026-09-09 BARCLAYS PREVIEW (Raimo Lenschow / Sheldon McMeans, Overweight, PT $250, px $162.52; block immediately below).** Barclays traded the print on two specifics plus one drawback. Tonight scores them:
- **① *"we think ORCL FINISHED ITS ATM"* → ✅ CONFIRMED, EXACTLY AS CALLED.** The **$20bn ATM was completed *"in entirety"* during Q1**. The *"much clearer FUNDING PICTURE"* Barclays was underwriting is real, and this was the highest-conviction leg of the call.
- **② *"expectations for NEARLY 1GW OF CAPACITY DELIVERED"* → ⚠️ CLOSE, BUT BELOW THE ROUND NUMBER: 850MW.** The same applies to DB's *"approaching 1GW"* (09-06), UBS's *"~1GW / 3x the normal run-rate"* (08-05) and JPM's ~1GW (06-16). 850MW is **~3x Q4 and 73% of ALL of FY26's deliveries**, so the direction is fully vindicated — but anyone who set 1GW as a hard bogey should mark it a **small miss on the capacity headline and a beat on everything that headline was a proxy for** (OCI +121% against +115–120% bogeys). ⚠️ Note the basis again: 850MW is stated *"since the end of Q4"* in prepared remarks and *"in Q1"* in Q&A.
- **③ *"the ONE DRAWBACK is the uncertainty around the NEW MEXICO DC, which could impact FY28+ numbers"* → ⚠️ DEFERRED, NOT RESOLVED.** Management ring-fenced **FY27** revenue and earnings guidance explicitly and **said nothing at all about FY28**; the **air permit is still outstanding**; and the **09-01 court stay went unmentioned**. Barclays' risk was always an OUT-YEAR risk, and the out-years were not addressed. **Score it UNCHANGED — the near-term-over-medium-term trade Barclays described is exactly what happened, which means the medium-term question is still sitting there at a $250 PT.**

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-05 — ASTRA WAS PRE-TRAINED IN THE ORACLE/OPENAI STARGATE DATA CENTER IN ABILENE, TEXAS, ON >100,000 GPUs — AND ORACLE'S OWN VP CONFIRMED IT.**
- 🔴 OpenAI said this was **"the first time they pretrained on MORE THAN 100,000 GPUs at our Stargate site in Texas."** **Oracle management identified the site as ORCL's Stargate data center in ABILENE, TX, running on [[NVDA]] GPUs** — sourced to a **LinkedIn post by Piyush Shah, VP, GPU and AI Infra, Oracle Cloud Infrastructure, 2026-09-05**, relayed by BofA's TMT desk (Brian Fenske, 2026-09-07 — *sales commentary, not research*).
- ➤ **Why this is a fundamental datapoint and not a press item: the single largest bear objection to ORCL is that the OpenAI relationship is a credit exposure dressed up as revenue. A frontier-model pre-training run of that scale COMPLETING SUCCESSFULLY on Oracle-built capacity is the first public, dated, technically specific evidence that the Abilene build is not just leased — it WORKS at frontier scale. It does not address the funding question at all.** ⚠️ **Source is a LinkedIn post relayed by a sales desk, not an ORCL filing or IR statement.**

**🆕🔴 2026-09-07 — THE FUNDING SIDE, RESTATED BY THE PRESS, AND THE NUMBER IS $48bn OF CASH BURN.** (The Information · Martin Peers, "The Briefing", 2026-09-07)

It also cited Oracle's expected capital expenditures forecast for this fiscal year, which is expected to lift its cash burn sharply. ANALYSTS ESTIMATE ORACLE WILL BURN $48 BILLION THIS FISCAL YEAR, DOUBLE THE AMOUNT FOR THE YEAR ENDING IN MAY, according to S&P Global Market Intelligence."**

Oracle has projected top-line growth of about 28% FOR THE FIRST QUARTER — compared with 21% in the May quarter — ACCELERATING TO 34% FOR THE FULL YEAR."**
- ➤ **Set the two side by side and the FQ1 print is a single question: does the revenue acceleration arrive on the same schedule as the cash burn? The 28% Q1 / 34% FY consensus frame is the total-company line and must NOT be confused with the ~115-120% IaaS bogey above — different bases, and conflating them is the fastest way to mis-score this print.**

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-06 — DEUTSCHE BANK'S F1Q PREVIEW: "FUNDAMENTALS IN-TACT, EXECUTION IN-FOCUS." DB IS THE FIRST HOUSE TO STATE THAT AN *OFFICIAL* ATM PROGRESS UPDATE LANDS **AT** THE PRINT — AND THE FIRST TO PUT A NON-GAAP **GROSS**-MARGIN NUMBER (61%) NEXT TO A NON-GAAP **OPERATING**-MARGIN NUMBER (−1pt y/y) FOR THE SAME QUARTER.** *(Deutsche Bank · **Brad Zelnick** w/ Bhavin Shah, Daniel Knauff, Yash Kejriwal — "Oracle — F1Q Preview: Fundamentals in-tact, execution in-focus", **2026-09-06**, 12pp. **BUY, PT $300 — DCF-derived, implies 34x DB's CY27E EPS.** Closing price cited in the note **$154.04**; shares **~−19% YTD**.)*

- **THE WORK BEHIND THE VIEW, AND IT IS MANAGEMENT-ACCESS WORK, NOT MODEL WORK:** DB spent time with Oracle senior management intra-quarter on a **July NDR** and at the **DB Tech Conference in August**, and came away *"feeling better about visibility to the ongoing capacity buildout, margins & underlying economics of contracted business, and broader cloud infrastructure market fundamentals."* Post-quarter checks were supportive of *"pricing tailwinds, simplicity and consolidation benefits from the new **'One Oracle' sales motion** and **breakout multicloud DB momentum**."*
- 🔴 **THE OVERHANG, IN DB'S OWN WORDS — and note it is the SAME two-part question this page has carried since 08-07, restated without softening: *"funding requirements to support **peak CapEx in FY27/28**"* and *"the timeframe for the cloud business to become **self-funding**."*** ➤ **The funding arithmetic and the ATM position are filed in `## Capex / financing / FCF strain` below, not duplicated here.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE FOCAL METRIC AND THE PHYSICAL CONSTRAINT UNDER IT — THIS IS THE CHECKABLE HALF OF THE NOTE. *"OCI revenue growth is the focal metric": **DB/Street forecast continued acceleration to +115% y/y @cc in F1Q, holding near that level in F2Q.*** And the reason it is a capacity print rather than a demand print: *"**the company has little spot or near-term capacity left to sell**"* — so the OCI number rests on execution against a large anticipated step-up in cloud capacity deliveries **"approaching 1GW" in F1Q, vs 400MW+ in EACH of the prior two quarters.** ➤ ✅ **This SITS ALONGSIDE, and corroborates, the marks already on this page: Jefferies' bogey grid (*"115% OCI growth"*, 09-01/02), BofA's model (*"IaaS growth of 116% YoY"*, 09-03/04) and management's own *"approaching 1GW"* on the Q4 FY26 call. Four independent sources now converge on ~115% and ~1GW — the F1Q OCI line has the tightest Street consensus of any number on this page.**
- ⚠️ **THE SWING FACTOR DB ADMITS IT CANNOT SEE, AND IT SHOULD BE HELD AS THE MAIN SINGLE-QUARTER RISK TO THE ABOVE: *"linearity of new capacity additions through the quarter"* is a potential swing factor **into which DB has limited view**. Its offset is a tracking claim, not a forecast: *"based on what we've heard/tracked, **everything appears on plan to deliver against FY27 guidance**."*** **Incremental opportunity per DB: quicker backlog conversion, recent pricing actions, and flow-through of **cost-plus contracts**.**
- **BEYOND OCI — THREE ITEMS DB IS WATCHING THAT THE PREVIEW FLOW HAS LARGELY IGNORED:** an uptick in **SaaS** revenue growth after multiple quarters of faster deferred-backlog build; ramping **VA site go-lives**; and management citing *"little-to-no evidence of slowing sales cycles"* in core **Enterprise and Government**.
- 🔴 **CLOUD DATABASE — DB IS WATCHING FOR A MARGIN-RICH INFLECTION, AND THE AVAILABILITY GATE IS NOW CLEARED: **multicloud DB has achieved GLOBAL REGIONAL AVAILABILITY across the three large CSPs**; management guided to **~40% y/y Cloud DB growth in FY27 at last year's FAM (+9pts y/y acceleration)**.** DB's checks in both areas were *"generally positive again this quarter."* ➤ **Read directly against the page's sharpest bear on exactly this line — Redburn (07-20, SELL) argues consensus leans on the database bet (~$20bn DB revenue by 2030 vs ~$3bn FY26) while cloud-DB grew only 29% y/y in Q4 FY26 against the 50%+ CAGR implied. DB's ~40% FY27 guide is the intermediate mark that adjudicates between them.**
- 🔴🔴 **PROFITABILITY — THE TWO-LINE MARGIN CALL, AND IT IS THE MOST DECISION-USEFUL NEW NUMBER IN THE NOTE. DB anticipates **another sizeable step down in non-GAAP GROSS margin to 61% in F1Q (−7+ pts y/y)**, from pre-revenue scaling costs and mix shift to AI infrastructure, **persisting through the year**. OpEx leverage plus recent cost actions are the offsets, supporting DB's forecast of only a **−1pt y/y drop in non-GAAP OPERATING margin in F1Q**. With management guiding **FY27 OpEx dollars DOWN SLIGHTLY y/y**, DB sees **NGOI growth ACCELERATING into the MID-20s for the year, from +16% in FY26**.** ➤ ⚠️ **DO NOT NET THIS AGAINST THE OTHER MARGIN MARKS ON THE PAGE WITHOUT CHECKING THE LINE: Jefferies' *"41% opm"* bogey and MS's *"Street F1Q operating margin 40.9%, ORCL's LOWEST IN 12 QUARTERS"* are OPERATING-margin marks; DB's 61% is a GROSS-margin mark. DB is the only house here forecasting BOTH, and its point is precisely that they move differently — the gross line takes the AI-infra hit while opex discipline holds the operating line nearly flat.**
- **DB'S STATED POSITIVE BIAS, verbatim:** *"AI Infrastructure backlog will be a highly value creative business over time, and complementary to opportunities in Core OCI, Cloud DB, and Applications."*
- ⚠️ **ONE UNRECONCILED MARK BETWEEN TWO HOUSES ON THE SAME PRICE, LOGGED NOT RESOLVED: DB (09-06) and BofA's desk (09-03/04) both cite a **$154.04** close, but DB puts the stock at **~−19% YTD** and BofA at **−21% YTD**. Same price, different YTD base. Neither is adopted as the page's YTD mark.**

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-04 — F1Q27 PREVIEW SEASON OPENED WITH THE TWO HOUSES FURTHEST APART ON THIS PAGE BOTH TURNING TACTICALLY POSITIVE, FOR THE SAME REASON: THE NEOCLOUD PRINTS REPRICED GPUaaS AND ORACLE HAS NOT RE-RATED. PRINT IS THURSDAY 2026-09-10, POST-CLOSE.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE CAPACITY ARITHMETIC IS THE ACTUAL CALL, AND IT IS CHECKABLE. Guidance is 58-64% y/y total cloud growth. MS's build: the midpoint implies **~$1.5bn of sequential OCI dollars and ~$7.3bn of total OCI revenue**; at **$10bn of ARR per GW** (Oracle's own illustrative contract economics from the October 2025 Analyst Day) that is **~1.6GW monetised at run-rate**. Back-solving the same math on MS's 4Q26 estimate gives **~1.1GW at run-rate exiting FY26 — slightly BELOW the 1.2GW+ management said it delivered in FY26.** Add the residual plus **half of the "nearly 1GW" management says lands in 1Q alone**, and MS gets **~63% y/y, i.e. the TOP of the guide.**** ➤ **This is the first place on the page where the guide is reconciled to gigawatts using the company's own $/GW constant. Anyone can re-run it on the print.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND HERE IS THE UNIT TRAP, WHICH THIS PAGE MUST NOT LOSE: MS's own note puts FOUR DIFFERENT $/MW MARKS SIDE BY SIDE, ALL REVENUE-PER-CAPACITY, NONE OF THEM BUILD COST.** **① Oracle's illustrative economics ≈ $10M/MW** ($10bn ARR/GW). **② [[NBIS]] mid-term contracts (~1-3yr) at ~$20-25M/MW.** **③ [[NBIS]] sub-6-month contracts trending to $40-50M/MW "and higher in some cases."** **④ [[CRWV]]: +5-10ppt contribution margin on 2Q-signed contracts and a ~25% price increase across SKUs, with older-generation ASPs holding at year-ago levels.** NBIS's first capacity auction in Q2 **cleared at the highest Blackwell-generation prices to date, 15% above prior peak**, and NBIS says Q2 deals **above $20M/MW with sub-two-year paybacks** come online from late Q4 and *"can serve as the baseline for pricing early next year."* ⚠️⚠️ **NEVER net any of these against a $/GW BUILD cost. And note the direction: Oracle's disclosed contract economics sit at HALF the cheapest neocloud mark and a QUARTER of the short-dated one.**
- ⚠️ **MS THEN TALKS ITSELF DOWN ON EXACTLY THAT POINT, AND THE REASON IS THE PAGE'S OWN 08-10 JEFFERIES MECHANISM: *"ORCL indicated at 4Q26 earnings that **98% of its AI Datacenter capacity is CONTRACTED**. We are unsure how much of the broader 10GW ambition that references, or over what duration, and as a result – **lean conservative on whether the inflection in AI capacity pricing can emerge as a material near-term upside driver.**"* MS expects the repricing to show up instead in *"longer-dated capacity and contracts signed intra-quarter, potentially showing up in **mid-CY27 results**."*** ➤ ✅ **This CORROBORATES the Jefferies structural argument already on this page (locked-in five-year unit economics forecloses participation in a 3-5x spot repricing) — from a different house, using different evidence, and reaching the same conclusion. Two independent confirmations now.**

Focus list: **(1) data-centre capacity deployment — including New Mexico and any delays, framing RPO-to-revenue conversion; (2) capex and funding requirements, including an update on the equity issuance programme; (3) core software trends.*** (BofA · Tal Liani via BofA TMT desk · Brian Fenske, sales commentary not research, 2026-09-03/04.)
- ⚠️⚠️ **SOURCE DEFECT, LOGGED AND NOT PROPAGATED: BofA's own two documents give TWO DIFFERENT SIZES FOR THE SAME EQUITY PROGRAMME — the 09-04 desk note says *"their (as of last qtr) **$30B ATM equity issuance**"*, while the 09-03 and 09-04 bogey polls both ask *"how much do you think they've completed out of the **$20B**."* **Neither figure is adopted; the page continues to carry the funding plan as previously sourced (DB's ~$20bn FY27 tranche within an ~$80bn FY27-28 need).** The desk's *"$240 (65% upside)"* also does not reconcile to its own $154.04 reference (which implies +56%).**
- **BUY-SIDE BAR, AS BofA IS POLLING IT — useful as the actual bogey list into 9/10:** Q1 total quarter-ending RPO · Q1 Cloud Infra (IaaS) revenue growth · Q1 operating margins · Q2 cloud revenue guide · Q2 EPS guide · **FY27 revenue guide (current guide $90bn)** · **FY27 EPS (current guide $8.05 non-GAAP)** · FY27/28/29 capex · completed equity issuance · bull/bear lean.
- ✅ **CHANNEL CHECKS — THREE ITEMS, TWO POSITIVE, AND THE MOST POSITIVE ONE IS NOT OCI.** ① **Multicloud database is *"the most consistent positive signal in our work"*: Database@Azure/AWS/GCP repeatedly cited, broader availability *"pulling some customers from evaluation into migration earlier than otherwise"*, one partner calling **Exadata inside the hyperscalers among the strongest parts of its Oracle business** — *"past awareness and pilots."* MS is careful: *"we would still distinguish this from broad OCI share gains; the opportunity is PROTECTING AND EXPANDING ORACLE'S DATABASE WALLET."*** ② **Cloud Applications stabilising**: improving qualified Fusion ERP/HCM discussions including renewed evaluations from customers weighing S/4HANA migration cost, **two consecutive quarters of deferred revenue growth ahead of reported Cloud Apps revenue**, and — importantly — ***"no material disruption in channel engagement following the large-scale RIF."*** ⚠️ **Offsets: limited new-logo activity at a reseller-oriented check; SAP's ECC support extension *"appears to have deferred part of the forced-decision catalyst"*; embedded application AI adoption *"muted"*, with one check seeing customers favour custom agents on external foundation models.** ③ ⚠️ **THE STRUCTURAL BEAR CHECK, VERBATIM: *"one check said **generic storage, networking and compute for custom applications still typically land elsewhere**, with another indicating that **greenfield data and AI-platform projects often favor competitors such as Databricks, [[SNOW]], Postgres or [[MDB]]**."*** ➤ **Core OCI remains anchored to Oracle workloads; the enterprise "AI factory" pitch (financial services, pharma) is real but early and *"GPU work remains a small share of activity."***
- **THE SENTIMENT MECHANISM MS ARGUES ORCL INHERITS: large-cap application SaaS names posting CY27 revenue revisions of ±1% after CY2Q26 results **outperformed software by ~18% over the following five days**, versus 4% underperformance on comparable revisions a year earlier — group EV/FCF entered the season **~60% below the 3Y average**. **WDAY +5.8% on +0.5% vs consensus; [[CRM]] +22.6% on +0.1%.** ⚠️ **MS is explicit that this does not carry ORCL on its own: *"in-line / slight upside to cloud results is NOT ENOUGH on its own to drive a positive post-print reaction, but should be complementary IF OCI DELIVERS."*** **What could go wrong, per MS: (a) weaker gross margins on the OCI capacity ramp, (b) an upward revision to capex.**


**🆕🔴 2026-09-03 — ORACLE SHOWS UP AS THE NAMED WIN IN [[HPE]]'s NETWORKING QUARTER: A 3-YEAR DEAL, AND THE VALIDATION IS ARCHITECTURAL.**
- **BofA · Wamsi Mohan (HPE F3Q26 review, 2026-09-03):** *"**The Oracle win (3-year deal) validates scale-out switching and scale-across routing.**"* Headline bullet: *"Oracle win validates scale-out switching and scale-across routing. HPE got a multi-billion agentic AI/inferencing order."* ➤ **Two separate items in one line — the Oracle networking deal and a separate multi-billion inference-server award (BofA sizes the latter at $3.5bn, from an unnamed hyperscaler, won POST-quarter). They should not be conflated.**
- **CONTEXT ON THE SCALE: HPE's networking orders grew +36% y/y (3.5x faster than the +10% normalised revenue), Networks-for-AI orders hit $700mn in the quarter with cumulative FY26 orders of $2.2bn, and HPE RAISED its year-end Networks-for-AI order target to $2.5-3.0bn from "at least $2bn"** (BofA; GS · Katherine Murphy, both 2026-09-03). ➤ **Oracle is a named anchor inside that raise.**
- 🔴 **WHY IT MATTERS FOR THE ORCL THESIS, NOT JUST HPE's: "scale-across routing" is the multi-site-cluster networking layer that [[ANET]] says already accounts for a third of its AI business and that [[CIEN]] is being bought for.** ➤ **Oracle buying a three-year scale-out/scale-across networking contract from a merchant vendor is a datapoint about the SHAPE of OCI's build — multi-site, interconnect-heavy, and not fully vertically integrated. It is corroboration that Oracle's capacity ramp is real and being contracted with third parties on multi-year terms.** ⚠️ **No dollar value, no capacity figure and no OCI disclosure — this is HPE's counterparty description of Oracle. Treat as directional.**
- **ALSO IN THE WINDOW (title-level only, body not pulled): Jefferies published *"Oracle (ORCL, BUY): Looking Past the Cloud of Doubt - See 50+ Page Deep Dive"* (2026-09-02) — a BUY-rated 50+ page deep dive whose thesis is explicitly a rebuttal of the cloud-scepticism case.** ➤ **Flagged for retrieval on the next ingest; no number adopted here.** **BofA's desk separately ran an *"ORCL & ADBE – Quick Bogey / Sentiment Poll"* into the print (2026-09-03) — the aggregated results did NOT arrive inside this window.**

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-02 — JEFFERIES CUTS ITS TARGET 9% AND STAYS BUY WITH THE MOST BEARISH-SOUNDING BULL NOTE THIS PAGE CARRIES: "PEAK-NEGATIVE SENTIMENT", A NEGATIVE-FCF BRIDGE OUT TO FY30, ~$260bn OF OFF-BALANCE-SHEET DC COMMITMENTS AND ~47% OF BACKLOG IN ONE COUNTERPARTY — AND IT STILL SEES +105% UPSIDE.** *(Jefferies · **Brent Thill** w/ Bo Yin, ShengQi Lin — "Looking Past the Cloud of Doubt", 50+ page deep dive w/ proprietary partner survey, published 2026-09-01, distributed 2026-09-02. **BUY, PT $290 — CUT FROM $320.** Spot **$141.32**.)*

- **THE TARGET AND THE MATH BEHIND IT:** **$290 = 17x FY30E EPS of $17**, itself *"a **20% discount** from [the company's] $21 target."* Jefferies FY27 quarterly EPS path **$1.76 / $1.86 / $2.07 / $2.39 = $8.08**; estimates **+1% vs consensus on FY27 revenue**, nothing else changed. ⚠️ **A 9% PT cut with NO estimate changes = a multiple/discount decision, not a fundamentals decision. Log it that way.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE FOUR OVERHANGS, IN JEFFERIES' OWN WORDS — this is the most complete statement of the bear case on this page and it comes from a Buy-rated analyst:**
  - **① FINANCING. *"Capex continues to ramp against negative FCF and already-pressured credit ratings. **FCF was −$24bn in FY26 and is NOT expected to turn positive until FY30E**."*** Balance sheet: *"**net leverage near historical highs around 4.5x** and a **rating-agency track record that could work against the company**."*
  - **② OFF-BALANCE-SHEET SCALE, SIZED: *"ORCL has accumulated roughly **$260bn in OFF-BALANCE-SHEET DATA CENTER COMMITMENTS, nearly 3x its FY27E capex of $90-95bn**."*** ➤ **Put next to [[NVDA]]'s $366bn + $56bn commitment tables and [[AVGO]]'s confirmed $29bn: the wiki now has all three legs of the AI-infrastructure off-balance-sheet stack sized by outside houses on the same day.**
  - **③ EXECUTION ON STARGATE: *"media reports speculating **delays at certain AI data center sites tied to LABOUR, PERMITTING APPROVALS and POWER AVAILABILITY**."*** ➤ **Same three constraints [[AVGO]] named as "land, power and shell" the same evening — and the opposite of [[NVDA]]'s IR line that power is *"not a serious FY28 concern."***
  - **④ CUSTOMER CONCENTRATION, QUANTIFIED: *"**~47% of ORCL's backlog is tied to a SINGLE leading AI lab**, raising questions about that counterparty's ability to fund a **$300bn contract** amid an increasingly competitive AI landscape."*** ➤ 🔴 **Read directly against [[NVDA]] IR the same day sizing [[OPENAI]] + [[ANTHROPIC]] at ~20% of NVDA's END-CONSUMPTION revenue, rising to ~25%. Oracle's single-lab concentration is roughly 2x NVIDIA's combined two-lab exposure, and Oracle's is contractual backlog rather than end consumption.**
- **THE F1Q27 BOGEY GRID — the numbers to score the print against:** *"Key bogeys: **115% OCI growth, 41% opm, 40% RPO growth**."* Detail: **F1Q is seasonally soft, but the Street's FLAT q/q total-revenue estimate is *"notably LESS CONSERVATIVE than the −6% and −7% declines in F1Q26 and F1Q25"***; **OCI growth expected to inflect to 115% y/y — a 22pt sequential acceleration on a 3pt TOUGHER comp (52% → 55%)**; **SaaS more tempered at 11% y/y**; **Street F1Q operating margin 40.9% would be ORCL's LOWEST IN 12 QUARTERS**, *"reflecting **GPU infra margin pressure** only partially offset by cost-cutting"*; **RPO Street $639bn (+40% y/y)**, with growth rates normalising *"as ORCL laps its flagship AI contract in F1Q27."*
- 🔴 **THE BULL ARGUMENT IS A SENTIMENT-AND-DOWNSIDE ARGUMENT, NOT A GROWTH ONE:** *"shares are **~41% BELOW their level ahead of the flagship AI contract announcement in early Sept 2025**, despite **accelerating growth through FY26 and $183bn of incremental RPO added over that span vs $138bn RPO pre-announcement**."* And the downside test: *"**even a ~30% MISS on ORCL's FY30E $21 EPS target (to ~$15) at 15x P/E implies a ~$225 stock, or ~59% upside**."* Thill's framing: *"this is **not a binary outcome**; the more likely path is that **some AI data center sites track on schedule while others see timelines pushed**."* Partner survey: *"a **q/q uptick in infra** and **steady apps growth**."*
- ⚠️ **THE HONEST READ OF THE NOTE: the stock is down 41% while RPO went from $138bn to $321bn. Jefferies' answer to "why is it down" is financing and counterparty risk, both of which it then argues are survivable rather than absent. That is a valuation call resting on a credit judgement, and it should be held as such.**
