- **⚠️ THE COST ITEM: "ORCL (Oracle) is preparing for ANOTHER ROUND OF LAYOFFS as the firm moves aggressively to SLASH EXPENSES amid the EXTREME EXPENSES ASSOCIATED WITH ITS AI INFRASTRUCTURE BUILDOUT"** (Business Insider, via Vital Knowledge / Adam Crisafulli, 2026-08-11). ➜ **Read this against the funding thread already on this page (DB's estimate that ~$40bn of incremental capital would be required in FY28): opex cuts to part-fund capex is the behaviour of a company whose AI buildout is outrunning its cash generation, and it is the same signal MS's desk describes across the group — "because of front-loaded investment, some of the major hyperscalers are now cash flow neutral or negative." Media-reported, not company-confirmed; no headcount or charge disclosed.**
- **🔴 THE DEMAND ITEM: Oracle is the consensus guess for the unnamed "top-five hyperscaler" behind Everpure's second Master Supply Agreement.** JPM: *"Everpure's announcement of a second major hyperscaler Master Supply Agreement with a 'top-five' hyperscaler (**likely ORCL**)"* (JPM / Joseph Cardoso, 2026-08-11). MS's Erik Woodring reasons to the same short-list from elimination: *"[[MSFT]] / ORCL are most likely partners — [[AMZN]] / [[GOOG]] focused on vertical integration and [[META]] already partner. Announcement referred to 'Top 5' Hyperscalers, which could be a nod to ORCL"* (MS TMT desk, 2026-08-11). **Deal shape, if it is Oracle: software and non-NAND components recognised as product revenue, NAND procured directly by the customer, support services ratable, with gross margins guided to 75–85% for the Hyperscale line.** ⚠️ **Neither Everpure nor Oracle has named the counterparty. Two brokers inferring the same name is a hypothesis, not a disclosure — and note the two houses disagree on whether MSFT or ORCL is likelier.**
- **NET-NEW PARTNERSHIP (confirmed, small): Quantinuum announced a multi-year strategic partnership to bring quantum computing to Oracle Cloud Infrastructure — "OCI customers will be able to directly access Quantinuum's Helios… through OCI's quantum service, alongside OCI's high-performance computing (HPC) and GPU infrastructure"** (Quantinuum Q2 release, via UBS / Robert Ruple, 2026-08-11). ➜ **Immaterial to revenue; logged because it is a differentiation datapoint for OCI's non-AI-training workload mix, which is the part of the OCI story that gets least attention.**
- **Capital-structure context: BofA's converts desk names ORCL among the significant AI-linked convertible issuers (alongside [[CRWV]], [[NBIS]], [[GOOG]], CIFR, WULF), arguing July's cheapening presents an entry point** (BofA / Michael Youngworth via Brian Fenske, 2026-08-11). **Relevant here mainly as evidence of how ORCL's AI capex is being financed and by whom.**

**Q4 FY26 (2026-06-10)** — record quarter. Revenue $19.2B, +21% USD (Maxson). Cloud infrastructure revenue +93%; Cloud Apps +10% ($4.1B, SaaS deferred +16%); cloud database +29% with multicloud revenue +404% YoY and bookings +325%. Non-GAAP op income $8.6B (+22%); non-GAAP EPS $2.11 (+24%, or +20% ex one-time investment gains). FY26: revenue >$67B, non-GAAP op income $29B (+16%), non-GAAP EPS $7.63 ($6.83 ex Ampere/Bloom gains); CFO $32B (+54%). Gross margin stepped down ~5pts FY26 as expected on data-center ramp, offset by opex efficiency (Maxson).

The headline is **RPO of $638B, +363% YoY** (Maxson) — 12% recognized in next 12 months, 34% in 13–36 months, both expected to accelerate. Magouyrk: signed **$67B in AI infrastructure contracts** in the quarter, majority bring-your-own-hardware (BYOH) or prepaid; BYOH/prepaid book now $75B at "no degradation in margin." Delivered >1.2GW in FY26; Q1 FY27 delivery "approaching 1GW," ~= prior four quarters combined. Global GPU utilization 97.5%; of 35k GPUs up for renewal across 59 customers, 49% of customers renewed 92% of GPUs and most non-renewed GPUs were resold same quarter. Five flagship sites detailed (Abilene TX 42% delivered; Shackelford TX; Doña Ana NM on Bloom fuel cells; Saline County MI; Port Washington WI).

**FY26 10-K review (filed 2026-06-23).** Incremental disclosures on the just-reported quarter: RPO net adds **+$85.4bn q/q** (implying >$100bn booked) to the $638bn total, recognition split 12% (next 12mo) / 34% (2-3yr) / 34% (3-5yr) / 20% thereafter; **cRPO ~+$12bn q/q to ~$79bn (~+73% y/y vs ~+65% prior quarter)**, implying ~88% coverage on NTM revenue guidance (closer to mid-90s ex transactional/License). Gross Computer/network/machinery & equipment assets reached **$60bn at FY-end (+97% y/y**, ~= F4Q OCI rev +92%), and **construction-in-progress (CIP) ~$40bn — up $23.5bn / +142% y/y** — signalling a large slice of FY26 capex not yet in revenue-generating service (~$40bn CIP ≈ the capex for 1GW of AI capacity), supporting the guided OCI acceleration. **Headcount exited FY26 at 141k, -21k / -13% y/y** (S&M -19%, R&D -14%, Cloud & Software -10%, Services -8%, G&A -8%; US -16%, Intl -12%), consistent with F4Q OpEx -5% y/y and guided down again in FY27 against >30% topline — the operating-leverage offset to mix-driven GM headwinds. DB reiterates Buy / PT $300 (DB/Zelnick FY26 10-K review, 2026-06-23).

**Guidance.** FY27: total revenue +34% cc (above the 5-yr CAGR); non-GAAP EPS $8.05, +18% cc ex one-time gains; net cash outlay for capex ~$70B (reported capex higher by ~$20–25B of prepayments/timing) — reaffirms Analyst Day +31% revenue CAGR / +28% EPS CAGR through FY30 (Maxson). Q1 FY27: total revenue +27–29% USD, cloud +58–64%, non-GAAP EPS $1.72–1.76 (+17–20%); H2-weighted as capacity comes online. Funding: ~$40B debt+equity in FY27 incl. the announced $20B ATM equity; no additional debt in CY26. Steady-state project ROIC "high 20s," better on BYOH/prepaid (Maxson, Q4 FY26). Prior quarter (**Q3 FY26, 2026-03-10**): RPO $553B, AI infra revenue +243% YoY, multicloud DB +531%; delivered >400MW (90% on/ahead of schedule); AI-capacity gross margin 32% vs 30%+ guide; database multicloud now live across 33 Azure / 14 Google / scaling to 22 AWS regions; TikTok US separation gave Oracle a 15% equity stake.

**FY30 Analyst-Day targets — the primary-source ladder (Financial Analyst Meeting, 2025-10-16).** The repeatedly-cited "+31% rev / +28% EPS CAGR through FY30" rests on an explicit non-GAAP target path that the page had not laid out: **total revenue $57B FY25 → $67B FY26E → $85B FY27E → $130B FY28E → $185B FY29E → $225B FY30E (31% CAGR)**, with **non-GAAP EPS $6.03 → $6.85 → $8.00 → $10.65 → $16.00 → $21.00 (28% CAGR)** (Doug Kehring, then-EVP/Principal Financial Officer). Worth holding against the live numbers: the FY27E $85B target vs. the FY27 guide of +34% cc off >$67B (~$90B), and the **FY28E $130B / FY29E $185B step-ups** that embed the OCI ramp the bears doubt.
- **This was an explicit *raise*.** The same meeting showed Oracle's prior-year (2024) targets were only **>$66B revenue / >10% EPS growth for FY26 and >$104B revenue / >20% EPS growth for FY29** — i.e. the new FY29E $185B is ~78% above the year-earlier FY29 target, the clearest single illustration of how fast the AI-infra demand re-rated management's own model in 12 months (2025-10-16).
- **RPO at the meeting: $455B, +359% y/y as of Q1 FY26** — the prior milestone on the path to the $553B (Q3) → $638B (Q4) figures already on the page; useful as the Analyst-Day anchor (2025-10-16).
- **Pre-AI revenue base for context:** total revenue compounded slowly pre-ramp — **$39B (FY20) → $40B → $42B → $50B → $53B → $57B (FY25)** (ex-Cerner growth only +2–9%/yr), and non-GAAP operating income **$17B (FY20) → $25B FY26E** — underscoring that the entire FY26→FY30 acceleration to $225B is AI-infra-driven, not organic-franchise (2025-10-16).
- **OCI-specific revenue ladder (Nomura "Is the cycle over", 2026-06-30, quoting Oracle):** distinct from the total-revenue target path above, Nomura reprises Oracle's own **Oracle Cloud Infrastructure** projection — **FY26 $18B (+77%) → FY27 $32B → FY28 $73B → FY29 $114B → FY30 $144B** — a useful OCI-only anchor to hold against the later Bernstein-cited **$166B FY30 OCI guide** on the page (i.e. the OCI FY30 number was subsequently taken up ~$144B→$166B). Nomura's ORCL comment set otherwise corroborates the on-page figures: FY27 net capex ~$70B (reported ~$20–25B higher on prepayments/timing), $67B AI-infra signed / $75B BYOH+prepaid book, RPO $638B (+363%), OCI 30–40% margin profile (Nomura "Is the cycle over", 2026-06-30).
- **Capital-allocation framework (mgmt's own words, 2025-10-16):** "pursue customers based on profit potential," "utilize diverse financing options to scale faster," "match expenses with revenue for datacenter builds," "relentless focus on cost discipline" — the framework now visible in the BYOH/prepaid structuring, the equity/ATM funding plan and the FY27 opex-down guide.

## The OpenAI deal — economics & concentration
The single contract that re-rated the stock. The OpenAI–Oracle commitment is framed as a **$300B / 5-year compute deal starting 2027 (~$60B/yr)** (Barclays on OpenAI, Nov25). Oracle's late-June disclosure that one large customer (assumed OpenAI) could spend **$30B/yr by FY28** anchors the bull/bear split — UBS sizes it at ~35% of OpenAI's projected FY28 revs ($86B) and ~2/3 of OpenAI's total compute spend if OpenAI runs ~50% of revenue on compute; a multi-year commitment to reach $30B by FY28 "could easily be $50-100B" (UBS OpenAI deep-dive). Roots: OpenAI already ran on OCI GPU racks via Microsoft's Azure off-loading, so OCI was the natural overflow once the Jan-2025 "Stargate" break in Azure compute exclusivity hit; the swing to Oracle "exceeded the most bullish investor expectations" (UBS OpenAI deep-dive).
- **Backlog/obligations context:** Of OpenAI's ~$650B in 10-yr compute obligations, **OCI is the single largest at $300B**, vs MSFT Azure $250B, GOOGL GCP $40B, AWS $38B, plus CRWV (Barclays on OpenAI, Nov25). Barclays' contract schedule has OCI's OpenAI revenue ramping $24B (2027) → $54B → $60B → $60B. Each ~1 GW cluster ≈ 400k GPUs; each GW generates ~$10B/yr of GPU rental revenue but costs $50–60B of capex to build (split compute vs power/shell) — the core capex-vs-opex mismatch driving the FCF strain.
- **Concentration risk, quantified:** OpenAI is a small slice of each hyperscaler's capacity except Oracle — Barclays models **OpenAI = 76% of OCI's '28 AI capacity** (vs 23% MSFT, 5% GOOGL, 3% AWS); OCI capacity 2.4 GW ('25 year-end) → 7.1 GW ('27). Oracle's offset is large books with ByteDance/TikTok, Meta and xAI (Grok inference) on top of OpenAI (Barclays on OpenAI, Nov25; UBS OpenAI deep-dive). UBS flags two live risks: (1) OpenAI building its own infra ("dig the dirt," OpenAI CFO) could relegate Oracle to a lower-value role; (2) the legacy Microsoft→Oracle sub-contract revenue could wind down as the direct OpenAI deal winds up, pressuring both Azure and OCI optics.
- **Unit economics (third-party view):** SemiAnalysis estimates Oracle signed **>$420B of OpenAI contract value over the trailing twelve months → ~$150B of gross profit**, but at an **AI ROIC of ~20%**, materially below Oracle's historical cloud profile and roughly in line with Microsoft's own AI ROIC once the OpenAI rev-share (ending ~2030–32) is stripped out (SemiAnalysis, Microsoft AI Strategy Deconstructed).

## Debate / thesis

**🆕🔴 2026-09-25 — PROJECT JUPITER, DAY TWO: THE HOUSES SPLIT ALONG EQUITY VS CREDIT, AND THE FT MOVES THE POWER RISK ONTO ORACLE.**
- **Equity says overreaction.** **Bernstein · Mark Moerdler** (daily summary + AM note, 09-25): the 4-5% drop is an overreaction. At current levels the valuation *"effectively excludes"* OCI AI revenue. New Mexico wasn't expected for customer use until late CY28, so **any delay wouldn't hit FY27/FY28 revenue**. The notice may give Oracle an option to cancel up to the contracted go-live date. DB's Buy/$300 (09-24) is already on the page.
- **Credit says overhang.** **MS · Lindsay Tyler / Vishwas Patkar et al** (*"Project Jupiter Drifts Off Orbit – The Gravity of Force Majeure"*, 09-25) **remains cautious on ORCL credit**. The notice is "somewhat surprising" given progress on two key approvals; it isn't a way to avoid debt-like obligations, since ORCL must deliver compute under GPUaaS. **FT (via @firstadopter, 09-25): Oracle guaranteed investor payouts even if the site has no power: "the power delivery risk sits with Oracle."** WSJ: **at least one lender sold project debt below 90c** of the **$18bn** bank loan. ORCL's "IG" bond closed at an **8.10% yield, in line with B3/B-** (@zerohedge, 09-24).
- **Rothschild & Co Redburn — Sell, PT $110 unchanged** (Street-low and the only Sell; via R&Co sales, Madeleine Falkiner, *"ORACLE (SELL) - Stargate Canary in the Coal Mine"*, 09-25). Redburn tracks **160+ AI data centers**; several ORCL/NBIS/CRWV sites screen *worse* than Jupiter on local opposition, grid and air permits. **ORCL has secured >10GW coming online over 3 years vs ~1.5GW today**, so the debate moves from unit economics to volume ramp.
- **Barclays (via @zerohedge, 09-25): ~$30bn of hardware capex tied to the site should slip with it**, typically spent 2-3 months before go-live. That is a read-through to NVDA and the ODMs. Rent is deferrable up to 3 years, but the lease extends to preserve total payments (WSJ/FT via TMTB). UBS sales (09-25): *"not good for the entire data center build-out thesis."*
- **Bull:** RPO/backlog is the entire bull case — $638B with contractual visibility, "the big get bigger" hyperscaler demand (Jefferies/GEV power call, 2026-05-08, noting Oracle's "half a trillion" backlog tracking Microsoft/Google). BYOH/prepaid model lets Oracle scale capacity without proportional cash capex or margin loss (Magouyrk, Q4 FY26). "Halo effect" — AI infra training colocated with apps/database drives multiproduct deals, sovereign/Alloy and multicloud DB at 60–80% margins (Sicilia, Q3 FY26). Execution proof point: DTE/Michigan site Oracle pulled a year ahead of a schedule DTE thought aggressive — a rare data-center *acceleration* vs an industry full of delays (Paul, Jefferies call 2026-05-08); SemiAnalysis corroborates — Oracle broke ground at Abilene in May 2024 and was operational by September, while Microsoft's competing Wisconsin Phase-1 was still not live two years after groundbreaking, so Microsoft's slow execution ceded the first ~$100B Stargate contract to Oracle (SemiAnalysis, Microsoft AI Strategy Deconstructed). Sell-side supportive: UBS (Keirstead) Buy, PT↑ to $285 "positive ahead of print, long-compute thesis" (briefing 2026-06-02) **⚠️ [PT SUPERSEDED — cut to $245 on 2026-08-05; rating still Buy. See Changelog]**; DB (Zelnick) Buy/$300 reaffirmed (briefings 2026-06-01/05/15).
- **Bear:** Capex-to-sales "at dotcom peak" (MS / Wigg, briefing 2026-06-09) — the single most cited risk; JPM models OCI capex intensity rising to **54% FY26 → 61% FY27 → 58% FY28**, with reported capex of ~$36B FY26E → $50B FY27E → $73B FY28E (JPM AI Capex & Financing, 2025-11-10). FY27 gross margin steps down again on data-center ramp/mix before recovering; near-term margin compression is real (Maxson). Massive external funding need (~$40B FY27, incl. $20B ATM equity dilution) at a time component costs — memory, SSD, HDD — are spiking (DiFucci/Guggenheim Q&A, Q4 FY26). Competitive flood: Neoclouds, SpaceX, CoreWeave "sold out at $99B backlog," hyperscalers self-building (Moerdler/Bernstein Q&A; Jefferies call). Power/transmission and construction-labor constraints are the gating factor industry-wide (Jefferies/GEV 2026-05-08). Stock was **-13.8%** the week into the 6/15 briefing despite the Buy reiterations — the market is voting on the cash-burn/margin profile, not the backlog.
- **Cloud-margin disappointment — the SemiAnalysis cross-CSP read (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth & Bedrock Mix", 2026-05-27):** in its cross-hyperscaler margin teardown, SemiAnalysis groups **Oracle (with CoreWeave) as having "disappointed the market with lower-than-expected profits from their cloud arms."** The structural point: the top hyperscalers' Token-as-a-Service businesses (AWS Bedrock, etc.) are $10B+ ARR and materially higher-margin, whereas Oracle's TaaS is "practically nothing" — ORCL is a wholesale AI-IaaS seller, the lowest-margin rung, which is why the margin gap between the top-3 hyperscalers and the rest (ORCL + neoclouds) is widening. Corroborates the on-page margin-compression bear and the ~20% AI-ROIC framing (note this is dated ~2 weeks before the Q4 FY26 print). (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth & Bedrock Mix", 2026-05-27). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_Bedrock.html)
- **Bear — "a massive blow": Oracle left the premium short-tenor compute segment to SpaceX (SemiAnalysis "Meta Compute: Everyone Wants To Be A Cloud", 2026-07-02):** the sharpest independent critique yet of ORCL's compute-*monetisation* execution — and note carefully that it is a critique of **pricing, not demand**. SemiAnalysis's AI-Cloud-TCO deal database (hundreds of GPU cloud deals/yr incl. SLA, pricing, tenor) shows SpaceX's Anthropic and Google compute deals priced at **3x and 4x, respectively, what peers charge in revenue per MW** — and because the cost structure is "roughly the same," the **profit-per-MW gap is larger still**; SpaceX's Google pricing is even above the on-demand/short-term rental market. Elon "essentially invented a new market segment": large-scale on-demand compute at a huge pricing premium, structured as a **3-year deal with a 90-day bilateral cancellation option — "effectively a 3-month deal with automatic renewal."** SemiAnalysis argues the financing burden **excludes every neocloud** from that market (large clusters need a multi-year offtaker), and the top-3 hyperscalers all passed because they had higher-value long-term options (Microsoft taking OpenAI IP for equity + compute; Amazon pushing Bedrock/Trainium adoption; Google pushing TPUs and Vertex, now Gemini Enterprise Platform). Conclusion: **"This effectively leaves only two companies that can truly capitalize on this new market: Oracle and Meta. For the former, we see this as a massive blow. It's yet another piece of evidence that they could've done a much better job at monetizing all of their gigawatts of compute"** — illustrated by the divergence in the valuation trajectory of **Oracle vs SpaceX**, for both of which gigawatts are an increasing and growing share of valuation. Read against the on-page ~20% AI-ROIC / wholesale-IaaS-lowest-margin-rung thread (SemiAnalysis 2026-05-27) and the BYOH-vs-comparable GM spread (WFC 06-11): the bear here is not that Oracle can't fill its gigawatts, it is that Oracle **priced them cheap on long tenors** while a new entrant proved a 3-4x revenue/MW segment existed. The mirror-image bull tell to watch: Oracle's own restraint framing — mgmt thinks in **8-10-year customer relationships** and won't overcharge short-term to avoid renewal problems (WFC, 2026-06-11) — i.e. the two views are the same fact seen from opposite ends of contract duration. (SemiAnalysis "Meta Compute: Everyone Wants To Be A Cloud", 2026-07-02) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_02_ENC_Meta_Compute_Everyone_Wants_To_Be_A_Cloud_email.html)
- **Small structural positive — infra discipline (SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21):** in a piece whose subject is Meta's over-engineering, Oracle is used as the mid-point of the networking-philosophy contrast: Amazon optimised relentlessly for cost (EFA on commodity Tomahawk), Google "treats networking as a software problem, and builds its own transport rather than buying one," Meta "paid for best-in-class, and then the definition of best-in-class changed" — while **"Oracle sits in between, building large, functional networks out of simpler hardware without trying to reinvent it."** A minor but genuine third-party endorsement of Oracle's infra capital discipline, and a useful counterweight to the monetisation critique above: the criticism of Oracle is about how it *sells* gigawatts, not how it *builds* them. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-21) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_21_ENC_Metas_Infrastructure_Team_Needs_A_Culture_Reset_email.html)
- **Frontier-lab dependence — token-optimization risk (third-party):** UBS flags that "software/infra firms such as Datadog and Oracle have outsized exposure to the success of the frontier labs and anything that weighs on the growth curve of the frontier labs could obviously dent investor confidence in these names" — i.e. the enterprise token-optimization wave (down-tiering to cheaper/open-source models, capping usage) is a read-through risk to ORCL given OCI's concentration in frontier-lab compute (OpenAI ~76% of '28 AI capacity) (UBS "The Ramp in Token Optimization" / Keirstead, 2026-06-23).
- **JPM "AI Capex 2.0" (2026-06-16):** frames ORCL as **the most reliant on external funding** in the hyperscaler cohort and "the clearest example where the trajectory depends on the revenue ramp arriving on schedule." Confirms F4Q: ~$19bn rev (+21%), OCI +93%, RPO $638bn (+$85bn q/q) on **$67bn of AI-infra contracts signed (4 customers each >$8bn)**, 1.2GW delivered FY26 / ~1GW in F1Q27, GPU util 97.5%, multicloud DB +404%. The funding nuance JPM stresses: a growing share of contracts are **customer-prepay or BYOH (now $75bn, the majority of the $67bn signed)**, prompting Oracle's new **net-capex metric ~$48bn FY26 → ~$70bn FY27 (reported capex ~$20-25bn higher)**; still leaves a **~$37bn FY27 funding gap before dividends**. Oracle raised **$43bn debt + $5bn mandatory-convertible preferreds in FY26** and plans **~$40bn debt+equity in FY27** (incl. the ~$20bn remaining on the ATM, of which only ~$5bn executed through May) — **the first hyperscaler to build equity into its capital plan, "more out of necessity than desire."** Against a ~$130bn gross-debt balance and **$261bn of lease commitments not yet on the balance sheet**; steady-state project ROIC framed "high 20s" (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16).
- **WFC Q4 FY26 callback (group w/ both co-CEOs + new CFO):** OCI is **sold out for the next six months and now selling 2027, not 2028** ("not sold out of everything we can do in 2027 but it's getting close"); ~quarter-to-quarter-and-a-half lag from capacity coming online to full revenue run-rate (rhymes with Azure timing) explains why F4Q OCI "missed" optically — read as **timing, not demand** (got to ~120% OCI growth on the Q1 guide). New CFO framed **fiscal 28 as the peak for dollar capex** (~$90–95bn FY27 → ~$150bn FY28 before stepping down FY29) on the current target model — "the semi guys did not like to hear" peak-capex timing; capex came in $90–95bn for the quarter. On pricing restraint in a supply-constrained market: Oracle thinks of an **8–10 year customer relationship** and won't overcharge short-term to avoid renewal problems. On the **4 customers each >$8bn**, mgmt would not confirm Anthropic was one, but praised Anthropic (Project Glasswing partner, "happy to offer Anthropic's APIs as part of OCI") and confirmed it **can now offer OpenAI APIs (incl. Codex) on OCI** after the Microsoft-exclusivity change. **BYOH gross margin ~55% vs ~40% on a comparable prior-structure contract** (illustrative, from mgmt meetings — Oracle takes very low margin on the chips); ~$25bn/yr used as the prepay/BYOH baseline. Gross margin down ~500bps FY26, another ~900bps guided FY27 before structural improvement; op margin held >40% on low opex growth. On the $20bn ATM: not yet executed (tied to the April CFO transition); next $20bn "could also be equity" (WFC "ORCL recap", 2026-06-11). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_11_orcl_wfc_recap_11_jun_26.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_11_orcl_wfc_recap_11_jun_26.html)
- **UBS (Keirstead) — Oracle as first to define peak capex:** Oracle "became the first of the large cloud providers to define when they're going to hit peak capex" (this fiscal year or next) — but Keirstead notes it's a moving target ("if Anthropic came to them a year from now to do a five-year, $100 billion deal," peak capex would shift later), and frames the hyperscalers (incl. ORCL) as increasingly acting as a **distribution endpoint for the frontier labs** — enterprises rolling out Claude Code "procure it through Azure or AWS," with no direct Anthropic commercial relationship, a lower-GM but revenue-accelerating + upsell-rich dynamic (UBS "Karl software call", 2026-06-18). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_18_karl_ubs_18_jun_26.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_18_karl_ubs_18_jun_26.html)
- **Jefferies (Brian) — contrarian ORCL defense:** frames Oracle's sequencing as sound — secured the OpenAI RPO first, then locked leases ("they have secured all the leases"), then gave the only multi-year guide of any public cloud ($20+ FY30 EPS power); MS (Weiss) skepticism (cutting that to ~$10) read as "a window into how skeptical people are willing to publicly doubt this company." Notes Dario/Anthropic did **a "$50 billion Fluidstack deal" while still a ~$1–2bn ARR company** — one of the most aggressively-sized compute deals relative to usage (Jefferies "Brent/Jeff AI" call, 2026-06-01). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_01_brent_jeff_ai_1_jun_26.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_01_brent_jeff_ai_1_jun_26.html)
- **Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter" (2026-06-22):** reiterates **Outperform, TP $325**; frames ORCL's shift from a Cloud story to a "major hyperscaler and AI training/inferencing provider" — **$638B RPO** (+363% y/y), **$166B FY30 OCI revenue guide**, led by **~$300B RPO from OpenAI**, as highly value-accretive over 5-10yrs despite near-term margin/cash pressure. Sees overhangs clearing as ORCL secures most/all financing needed for current demand by **end of FY27** (FY27 raise ~$40B = $20B announced equity + ~$20B debt/equity TBD; no further debt in CY26). New CFO framing OCI AI-datacenter ROIC "in the 20s"; component-cost increases largely passed through via contract terms so gross-profit dollars not meaningfully impacted (Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter", 2026-06-22).
- **Bernstein "Scaling Intelligence: The Generative AI Handbook" (2026-06-22) — framework, not fresh print:** the 247-page compendium dedicates a full chapter ("The Shape of Oracle: Understanding the Business in Five and 10 Years, FY25-FY35") to ORCL as the case study for AI IaaS-PaaS economics. Core thesis pieces relevant to the long: (1) **OCI on track to become potentially the third-largest global hyperscaler**, driven by mega AI-DC contracts; (2) the IaaS-PaaS model has an investment phase and a value-return phase — one must look out to **FY29+ to see the value created during the FY26-FY30 build**, when revenue/OI/FCF "could increase many times"; (3) Bernstein cites Oracle's guide of **30-40% gross margins on OCI AI revenue** (used by OpenAI, Meta and others) as evidence scale + unique IP lift infra margins well above pure-play neoclouds (CoreWeave ~24% GAAP GM FY24) — and notes ORCL has already "rapidly improved AI margins over 2024-26"; (4) on the apps side, SAP and **Oracle are framed as "best positioned"** among SaaS incumbents to survive the AI disruption (Oracle SaaS embeds AI at no incremental cost, raising product pricing over time); (5) AI accelerates cloud migration → high-triple-digit growth in Oracle multicloud database (on Azure/AWS/GCP). Thematic reinforcement of the existing bull, no new quarterly numbers (Bernstein "Scaling Intelligence", 2026-06-22).
- **Oracle as the cautionary comp — the tape now punishes an AI-infra capex raise, and UBS says so out loud (UBS · Karl Keirstead / Taylor McGinnis / Jack Fyda "Microsoft Corp. — Update on Azure, Capex and M365", 2026-07-26):** read-through from the UBS MSFT pre-print note, in which Oracle is used three separate times as the negative precedent. **(1) The Street's revised reaction function:** "We're eyes-wide-open to the reality that **the Street is not rewarding the stocks of AI infra providers that signal higher capex spend and/or don't post upside to cloud revs growth and margins (see Oracle and Google)**." Applied to Microsoft's own set-up: investors being pre-worried about a capex raise "obviously helps the stock set-up, **although we'd counter that this was true into both the recent Google and Oracle prints and capex raises from both were not greeted with applause**." This is the cleanest external statement of the mechanism the ORCL page has been describing from the inside — the backlog is conceded, the capex is the tax. **(2) Frontier-lab concentration is now the shared risk factor, and UBS names Oracle alongside Microsoft:** "Given the consensus view that open source (Chinese and other) AI models are poised to take share from the frontier labs, investor sentiment about Microsoft's high OpenAI exposure has swung back to being perceived as a risk, **contributing to the weakness in the shares of the hyperscalers with large frontier lab exposure (Microsoft, Oracle)**," and separately: "in our view this is a key reason why the shares of **Microsoft, Oracle, CoreWeave and other AI infra providers have been weak since early-June** when these issues first arose." Dates the de-rating precisely to **early June** and attributes it to open-model share gains against the frontier labs — a different cause than the financing/dilution bear that dominates this page, and one that is orthogonal to it. **(3) Oracle's equity transaction is now the reference point for whether Microsoft has to raise:** "**The Google and Oracle equity transactions have raised questions as to whether Microsoft may be forced to raise external capital**" — UBS attaches a low probability to MSFT going to the equity market because "Microsoft shares are almost 30% off its highs," which by construction says the ORCL/GOOG raises are read as price-dependent and Oracle raised from a weaker position. **(4) The genuinely two-sided item, and it cuts AGAINST the Redburn Sell on this page:** in the margin section UBS writes that Microsoft's GM fell only 110bps y/y despite $37bn (11% of revs) of AI revenue, and — the read-across — "**We've made the same observation about the margin performance at AWS, Oracle and others — on the surface, AI capex/revs are not landing at the abysmal margin levels that the bears feared.**" That is a direct, attributed contest of Redburn's "Gen-AI gross margins sit well below the 30% investors treat as a trough" thesis (07-20, SELL/$110) and of the SemiAnalysis "wholesale AI-IaaS is the lowest-margin rung" framing (05-27), from a house that is Buy-rated on Oracle at $285 **(as of 07-26; UBS has since cut the PT to $245 on 2026-08-05 while keeping Buy — the margin argument below was NOT retracted in the 08-05 note, which instead published a 10% fully-loaded ROIC of its own)**. Logged as disagreement, not resolved. **(5) Valuation marks from UBS's peer table (prices 7/24/2026):** ORCL **$114.99, -41% YTD, market cap $335,196m**; revenue growth **MRQ +20.6% / CY26E +25.9% / CY27E +38.7%** — the highest CY27E growth in the twelve-name comp set — on non-GAAP P/E of **15.4x CY26E / 12.6x CY27E**, versus a peer-group mean of 16.1x/14.4x and MSFT at 19.2x/16.9x. i.e. Oracle screens as the fastest-accelerating and among the cheapest names in the group, which is the quantified version of the page's central tension. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ued48088.html)
- **Where the sell-side/buy-side stands:** Clustered bullish but watching margins. **DB (Zelnick) Buy, PT $300 — REITERATED 2026-09-06 in the F1Q preview, and the target is now stated with its method: DCF-derived, implying 34x DB's CY27E EPS (ref $154.04, shares ~−19% YTD).** DB's modelled OCI growth is now **+115% y/y @cc for F1Q** on capacity deliveries **"approaching 1GW" in F1Q vs 400MW+ in EACH of the prior two quarters** — ⚠️ **superseding the June-briefing marks of ~+89% y/y modelled OCI growth and ~400MW/qtr, moved to Changelog 2026-09-06.** Margins are still "under the microscope," but DB now splits the line: **non-GAAP GROSS margin 61% in F1Q (−7+pts y/y, persisting through the year) against only −1pt y/y on the non-GAAP OPERATING margin, with NGOI growth accelerating to the mid-20s from +16% in FY26** (briefings 2026-06-01 → 2026-06-15, incl. post-virtual-IR "strong cloud DB/RPO positioning"; "F1Q Preview: Fundamentals in-tact, execution in-focus", 2026-09-06). **UBS (Keirstead/Sultan) Buy — PT CUT to $245 on 2026-08-05 ("Key OCI Debates, Where We Stand", px $144.28), from $285 (which had itself been raised from $250 on 2026-06-02; $285 superseded — see Changelog).** The cut is purely valuation: estimates held (UBS EPS 05/27E $8.14 / 05/28E $10.82 vs cons $8.06/$11.02) while the target multiple went from **28x C27e to 19x C28e** non-GAAP EPS — "a premium to the peer group, reasonable for 30%+ revs growth." **⚠️ Note the pattern: UBS (08-05) and JPM (08-13) both maintained their positive rating, both cut the multiple, and both rolled the valuation year forward — so the sell-side's *stated* stance on ORCL is more bullish than its *implied* discount rate. The PT range on the page is now Bernstein $325 / DB $300 / Mizuho $320 / UBS $245 / JPM $200 (Dec-27) / Redburn $110.** **Mizuho (Rakesh) Outperform, PT $320** (2026-07-11), naming ORCL a key neocloud/OCI capex driver. **First bear-rated note now on the page: Rothschild & Co Redburn (Haissl/Han) SELL, PT cut to $110 from $155 (px $124.21)** in "Hyperscalers — Margin Call" (2026-07-20) — group gross margin fell 510bp; consensus implies mid-30s OCI gross margin leaning on the database bet (~$20bn database rev by 2030 vs ~$3bn FY26) but cloud-DB grew only 29% YoY in Q4-FY26 vs the 50%+ CAGR implied, and consensus assumes the opex ratio falls to ~15% by 2030; RDBN EPS FY27/28/29 $5.88/$7.42/$10.30 vs cons $6.21/$9.01/$13.17. Otherwise the bear case lives in the MS capex-intensity flag, JPM's credit/leverage work, and the price action. **On the credit axis, JPM (Anmuth/Chatterjee/Speer, 2026-07-20) UPGRADED ORCL credit to overweight** — the weakest name (low-triple-B; S&P cut to BBB- stable, Moody's negative) but with ~$25bn YTD debt done, no return to the US debt market in CY26, $150bn of leases signed in one quarter late last year, and ~$20bn of equity still needed (issuance an overhang awaiting a strong RPO print). BofA (Post, 2026-07-20) stays bearish: RPO conversion only 12% over the next 12 months (vs MSFT 25%), still needs ~$20bn equity + more debt over 3 years, targets 1GW→10GW over 5 years while guiding capex to "plateau" (a disconnect), Abilene ~77% complete, "no new catalyst unless they convert more RPOs" — balance-sheet/dilution risk dominates (px ~$120).

## Capex / financing / FCF strain
The balance-sheet story is now central, not incidental (JPM AI Capex & Financing, 2025-11-10):
- **🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-06 — DEUTSCHE BANK PUTS A RUNNING TALLY ON THE FUNDING PLAN FOR THE FIRST TIME, AND IT IS THE CLEANEST STATEMENT OF WHERE THE CAPITAL PROGRAMME ACTUALLY STANDS GOING INTO THE PRINT** (DB · **Brad Zelnick** w/ Bhavin Shah, Daniel Knauff, Yash Kejriwal, "Oracle — F1Q Preview: Fundamentals in-tact, execution in-focus", 2026-09-06, 12pp; **Buy, PT $300**, ref $154.04):
  - **THE ANCHOR NOBODY HAS BEEN QUOTING: *"prior management last attempted to RINGFENCE incremental capital needs in DECEMBER at **<$100bn**."*** ➤ **That is the benchmark every subsequent estimate on this page should be scored against — UBS's ~$345-350bn of FY26-30 capex (08-05), DB's own ~$80bn FY27-28 incremental financing (08-07), JPM's ~$40bn FY27 + ~$20bn/yr thereafter (08-13). The December ringfence was a CAPITAL-NEEDS statement, not a capex statement; it has not been formally re-stated since, and the F1Q print is the first natural venue to do so.**
  - **WHAT HAS ACTUALLY BEEN RAISED SINCE: a **net ~$28.5bn from DEBT AND CONVERTIBLE PREFERRED issuance**.** ⚠️ **BASIS FLAG BEFORE THIS IS NETTED AGAINST ANYTHING ELSE ON THE PAGE: the credit desks track ORACLE'S BOND ISSUANCE at **$25.0bn from a SINGLE February deal** and $0 foreign-currency (Barclays FICC 08-20; MS Global Credit 08-20, same figure independently). DB's ~$28.5bn is a NET figure across debt *and convertible preferred* — a wider perimeter than the straight-bond tally, and the two are consistent rather than contradictory. Do not add them.**
  - **THE FORWARD PLAN, RESTATED: intentions to raise **$40bn via a combination of debt + equity in FY27**, including the closely watched **$20bn ATM equity program** — and **DB expects an OFFICIAL PROGRESS UPDATE ON THE ATM WITH F1Q RESULTS.*** ➤ **This upgrades DB's own 08-07 language on this page (the program was described only as *"in progress"*, with *"F1Q earnings disclosure is likely the next opportunity for a more specific update"*) from *likely* to *expected*. It also gives the $20bn figure a third independent house — against BofA's desk, whose own two documents give $30bn and $20bn for the same programme (source defect already logged 09-04; still not adopted).**
  - **DB'S DOWNSIDE ARGUMENT ON THE MIX, carried over from its prior *"Flexing Funding Mix"* report: *"all else equal, additional downside risk from **financing mix alone** is limited at these levels."*** ⚠️ **Note the precision of the claim — it is about the DEBT-vs-EQUITY MIX at the current share price, not about the SIZE of the need. It does not rebut the leverage bear (Jefferies' ~4.5x net leverage / FCF negative to FY30E, 09-02) or the spread bear (MS Credit 08-14, Barclays 08-20); it only says the choice between funding channels is close to sign-neutral for the equity here.**
  - 🔴🔴 **THE CAPACITY-DELIVERY STEP-UP, WHICH IS WHAT THE FUNDING IS BUYING AND THE VARIABLE THE PRINT ACTUALLY TESTS: DB expects cloud capacity deliveries **"approaching 1GW" in F1Q, versus 400MW+ in EACH of the prior two quarters** — with *"the company [having] little spot or near-term capacity left to sell."* **Linearity of those additions through the quarter is a swing factor DB explicitly says it has limited view into**, offset only by a tracking claim: *"everything appears on plan to deliver against FY27 guidance."*** ➤ **This is a ~2.5x sequential step-up in delivered megawatts being underwritten with capital that is not yet fully raised. That adjacency — the ATM update and the 1GW delivery number landing in the SAME disclosure — is why the F1Q print is simultaneously an equity event, a credit event (MS Global Credit's named single-name watch, 08-20) and a capacity event.**
- **FCF turns deeply negative through the build:** FCF –$0.4B FY25A → **–$9.2B FY26E → –$14.0B FY27E → –$19.8B FY28E** (FCF conversion ~–35% to –40%), driven by capex ramping $21B FY25 → $36B FY26E → $50B FY27E → $73B FY28E even as op cash flow grows to ~$52B FY28E.
- **Most-indebted IG tech issuer:** the $18B six-tranche senior-note deal (Sept-25, ~$88B order book) lifted total debt above $100B, making Oracle the most indebted IG-rated Technology issuer and 5th-largest in IG TMT. JPM models gross debt $121B FY26E → $151B FY27E → $171B FY28E; net leverage ~3.1x near-term before deleveraging to ~2.5x FY28E as EBITDA scales. JPM reads the strong $18B order book as scarcity of AI-related IG paper, not a vote on Oracle credit quality; ORCL spreads widened ~30-40bps post-issuance.
- **Forced into private/structured credit:** with traditional capacity constrained by negative FCF and elevated leverage, JPM expects Oracle to pursue Beignet-/Vantage-style off-balance-sheet structures and possibly hybrids (perpetual subs / preferred) for partial equity credit. Caveat: Oracle's mid-BBB ratings with Negative outlooks (S&P, Moody's) make a Meta-Beignet-like notched structure harder; sustained reliance on private credit "could signal funding flexibility is eroding."
- **Oracle as the first mover in the hyperscaler shift to debt — and the size of the market it implies (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop Unleashes the AI Project Trinity", 2026-07-06):** third-party corroboration of the on-page financing bear, and it names Oracle first: "up to now the majority of AI buildouts have been primarily cashflow funded by the hyperscalers such as Google, Amazon, Meta, Microsoft, Oracle. Over the last year, that's started to turn with **Oracle then Meta, and now even Google turning to debt.**" The framing around it is the bull-for-financeability side: SemiAnalysis models AI debt financing growing from hundreds of billions in 2024-25 to a **~$7.1T outstanding credit market by 2029**, second only to the ~$13T US MBS market, with annual AI capex above **$2T in 2028** — i.e. the funding gap the ORCL bears price is expected to be met by a market that scales to it, not by a market that refuses it. The constraint sequence SemiAnalysis lays out (2025 datacenter capacity → early-2026 chip production → **mid-2026 financing**) is exactly where Oracle sits today. Two structural datapoints that read in Oracle's favour on the *counterparty* axis: datacenter operators "do not see the incentive to rent to Neoclouds when they can happily do **10-15-year offtakes with a hyperscaler like Microsoft directly**" — Oracle's IG-rated, long-tenor offtake is the paper landlords want; and **neocloud colocation leases carry a 3-5% higher yield-on-cost** than hyperscale/investment-grade deals (weaker credit + forced amortisation/cash sweeps, often ~3-yr refinance windows), i.e. Oracle's cost of leased capacity is structurally below a neocloud's. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-06) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_06_ENC_Nvidia_GPU_Debt_Backstop_Unleashes_the_AI_Project_Trinity_Capital_Offtake_and_Datacenters_email.html)
  - **AMD-side exposure worth noting on OCI:** the same note records that **AMD has been running vendor backstops since 2025 — offering AWS, OCI, DigitalOcean, Vultr, Tensorwave and Crusoe a deal to rent capacity back for internal software development if the operator cannot sell it.** A small de-risking of Oracle's AMD Instinct footprint (a vendor put on unsold AMD capacity), and the precedent for the far larger Nvidia backstop program the note is actually about. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-06)
- **Off-balance-sheet lease load — "funding relief, not resolution":** the FY26 10-K (year ending 5/31/26) confirms ORCL's **not-yet-commenced lease commitments plateaued at ~$260bn** (MS expected the plateau), while the total across the five IG players rose ~$675bn → ~$820bn. On-balance-sheet lease liabilities grew **>$10bn net in FY4Q** with treatment still skewed operating (not finance). QoQ ALSO: deferred revenues +$4.2bn, unpaid capex +$0.8bn, purchase obligations (primarily DC power) +$2.3bn, short-term lease guarantees +$1.1bn. Post-FY26, Oracle added **$19bn of cloud-infrastructure purchase commitments (FY27-start, 5-year) that appear NOT to meet lease treatment** — i.e. more obligation building outside the lease line (MS TMT Credit, 2026-06-22). DB's read of the same 10-K: balance-sheet lease liabilities +$10bn q/q to **~$38bn**, total lease commitments incl. not-yet-commenced +$9bn q/q to **~$298bn**, with F4Q lease opex ~$1.0bn = ~17% of PaaS/IaaS revenue, in line with peers (DB/Zelnick FY26 10-K review, 2026-06-23).
- **★★ THE AUGUST TRIANGULATION — three houses sized the funding need within nine days, and the disagreement is smaller than the noise suggests.** Setting the three side by side is the most useful thing on this section right now:
  - **UBS (Keirstead/Sultan, 2026-08-05):** capex FY25 $21.2bn → FY26 $55.7bn → **FY27 $96.6bn → FY28 $109.7bn → FY29 $74.8bn → FY30 $62.8bn** (above the Street's $92bn FY28, below its $88bn FY29 — the peak-capex guide expressed mechanically). Cross-checked two ways to **~$345-350bn of capex FY26-FY30**: (1) IaaS $18.1bn FY26 → **UBSe FY30 IaaS $157.3bn** at "$2.5 of capex per dollar of future revs"; (2) "10+ GW of power and data center capacity coming online over the next three years" at a warm-shell-adjusted **~$35bn/GW to Oracle** (vs a Crusoe-sourced all-in $57.2m/MW). Funding verdict: FY27-30 OCF **$308bn** vs reported capex **$344bn** = a **~$36bn gap**, ">$45bn of negative FCF in each of the next 2 years" flipping positive in FY29 — but "**if** Oracle continues to negotiate contracts with BYOH and cash pre-pay provisions… **highly likely that there will be NO CASH FUNDING GAP over the full FY27-FY30 period**… [while] there **most certainly IS** a funding gap for FY27 and FY28." Leases converted to a P&L number: **15+ year average duration, so $260bn of obligations ≈ ~$17bn of annual lease expense, much of it through COGS** — and the commitments "have begun to LEVEL-OFF post the big 2Q/Nov 2025 jump, perhaps a signal that Oracle is focused on executing already planned builds rather than taking on material new construction."
  - **Deutsche Bank (Zelnick, 2026-08-07):** **~$40bn of incremental capital in FY28** on **~$120bn gross capex** and $20-25bn of customer prepayments, "probably toward the high end"; **~$80bn of FY27-28 incremental financing** flexed 100%-debt (base) vs 100%-equity (bear) at issuance prices of **$150/sh and $100/sh**, share prices derived as FY30 EPS × multiple discounted at a **12% cost of equity**.
  - **JPM (Chatterjee, 2026-08-13):** gross capex **~$95bn FY27 → ~$120bn FY28 → ~$133bn FY29 → ~$140bn FY30**; capital raises **$40bn FY27 (modelled 50:50 debt/equity) + $14bn FY28 + $20bn FY29 + $7bn FY30 ≈ ~$20bn/yr**, with the admission that "our capex forecasts are **higher than those implied by the management team**." Gross debt **$129.5bn** at 4Q26 (from $75bn in FY22), net leverage **3.3x — the highest amongst the hyperscalers**; net debt to **$140bn end-CY27 / $171bn end-CY28**; FCFF **-$23.7bn FY26 / -$35.1bn FY27 / -$27.7bn FY28**; embedded share count **2,914m → 3,167m by FY28 (~8.7% dilution)**.
  - ➜ **What to take from it: all three sit ABOVE the Street on FY28 capex, all three locate the gap in FY27-FY28, and none of them treats it as open-ended. The variable that decides whether this is a two-year bridge or a structural hole is the BYOH/prepay share of new contracts — which is also the variable Oracle controls least, because it is a customer concession.**
- **Credit: S&P's downgrade triggers are now explicit and testable (UBS, 2026-08-05).** After the mid-July cut to **BBB-** ("the lowest investment-grade rung and one notch above high-yield," per JPM dating it **2026-07-09**) on customer concentration, capex and memory inflation — with S&P conceding it "underestimated the scale of the investments required" — the agency named three triggers for a further downgrade: **(a) leverage sustained above 4.5x, (b) a view that Oracle is not on a path to be FCF-POSITIVE BY FY29, (c) a negative change in S&P's view of the AI strategy or industry.** Ratings row: Moody's **Baa2/Negative**, S&P **BBB-**, Fitch **BBB/stable** (vs [[MSFT]] AAA, [[CRWV]] B+). UBS's framing of why the balance sheet holds at all: total debt **$130bn** / net **~$100bn** against a **43% FY26 non-GAAP operating margin and a 48% OCF margin** — "Oracle can absorb high levels of debt because of its hugely-profitable database software business, its cash flow engine… Oracle remains IG, but is **teetering on the edge**." ➜ **JPM makes the same point from the other side: the non-Cloud half of Oracle ($33.4bn FY26, ~50% of revenue, +1% y/y) is "the cash generation engine funding in part the capital-intensive infrastructure build-out." Trigger (b) — FCF positive by FY29 — is the single line item to monitor, and it happens to be exactly where UBS's model turns.**
- **🔴🔴 THE CREDIT DESK'S VERSION OF THE SAME BEAR, AND IT IS THE SHARPEST STATEMENT OF IT ON THIS PAGE BECAUSE IT CLOSES BOTH FUNDING CHANNELS AT ONCE (Morgan Stanley US Credit Strategy · Vishwas Patkar / Fernanda Lima / Vishwanath Tirupattur / Carolyn Campbell / Eva Baurmeister / Christina Sigler, "A Break from the Heat, For Now", 2026-08-14).** Verbatim: *"wider spreads alone might be insufficient for lower-rated companies to maintain access to the credit markets. **Our credit sector analysts remain cautious on Oracle given its forward financing needs, with bond yields approaching 8% and the stock down ~22% YTD, reducing the attractiveness of future equity raises as a source of funding.** Here, high debt costs might work as a natural stabilizer for future issuance as the market awaits more clarity."* ➜ **This is the equity-and-credit doom loop stated explicitly, and it is a FINANCING-CAPACITY constraint rather than a spread observation: bonds near 8% make debt expensive at exactly the moment a stock down ~22% YTD makes the $20bn ATM equity leg dilutive. The page has carried "$40bn FY27, modelled 50:50 debt/equity" (JPM, 08-13) as if the mix were a choice — MS credit's point is that BOTH legs are impaired simultaneously, so the mix question may resolve by neither channel being cheap.** ⚠️ **Reconciliation of the YTD marks, because the page carries two and they look contradictory: UBS's peer table had ORCL **-41% YTD at $114.99 on 2026-07-24**; MS has **~-22% YTD on 2026-08-14**. Both are consistent with the same starting price (~$195) — the stock rallied to ~$153 by 08-12 (JPM's price mark), which arithmetically moves YTD from -41% to ~-22%. Not a data conflict; a dated one.** ⚠️ **Note also what MS credit does NOT say: it does not claim the market is closed. UBS's 08-05 framing ("still open… but spreads are wider and appetite has waned somewhat… a HIGHER INTEREST EXPENSE DRAG BUT NOT A CREDIT AVAILABILITY RISK") and MS credit's "high debt costs might work as a natural stabilizer" are the same observation with opposite emphasis, and both are on the page.**
  - **The note's broader thesis, which is the constructive half: medium-term AI financing needs remain SUBSTANTIAL despite the potential for a near-term slowdown after a busy summer; financing should shift INCREASINGLY TOWARD CHIPS AND COMPONENTS, with a larger role for private capital and high-quality credit support; balance-sheet and market capacity remain ample but increasingly price-sensitive — "making wider spreads the natural release valve." MS favours COLLATERAL-BACKED RISK.** ➜ **Read against Oracle's own structure: ORCL is the least collateral-backed of the large AI borrowers (its offtake IS the collateral story, and its $260bn of not-yet-commenced leases sit outside the balance sheet), which is exactly the axis MS says the market is starting to price.**
  - **YTD spread change (bp) by bucket, for scale: HQ Hyperscalers +29 · Hyperscalers incl. ORCL and [[SPCX]] +26 · [[NVDA]]+[[AVGO]] +20 · Semis +2 · Tech +11 · IG +1** (MS US Credit Strategy, 2026-08-14). ⚠️ **The hyperscaler bucket that includes Oracle widened 26bp while broad IG widened 1bp — the credit market is differentiating within tech, not repricing tech.**
- **🔴 THE FOLLOW-UP TRACKER SIX DAYS LATER — SUPPLY HAS NOT SLOWED, AND ORACLE'S ENTIRE YTD ISSUANCE WAS ONE FEBRUARY DEAL (Morgan Stanley Global Credit Strategy & Securitized Products · Fernanda Lima / Carolyn Campbell / Vishwanath Tirupattur / Vishwas Patkar / James Egan / Aron Becker / Jonathan Loke / Mayank Verma / Lindsay Tyler / Kate Konetzke, "AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider", 2026-08-20).**
  - **The issuance picture: HQ hyperscalers ~30bp wider YTD, yet $54bn priced in July and $41bn in August month-to-date; ~$445bn of issuance across credit channels YTD versus $217bn for FULL-YEAR 2025, against an FY26E of $500–550bn.** MS expects *"a busy fall and continued spread widening"*, and beyond public markets says HQ issuers *"will continue to flex their ratings muscles to provide credit support to others"* — the mechanism this page already carries from the vendor-backstop side.
  - 🔴 **THE ORACLE-SPECIFIC LINE ITEM, and it is the one to hold against the ATM debate: YTD-2026 issuance by bucket (US$bn) — HQ Hyperscalers 132 · ORACLE 25 · [[SPCX]] 25 · Semiconductors 42 · REITs 5 · Data Center Construction 43 · total IG 271. The monthly hyperscaler path Jan→Aug runs 0, 20, 37, 25, 0, 0, 25, 25. ORACLE'S $25bn WAS A SINGLE FEBRUARY DEAL** (vs $8bn in the prior-year period, **+223%**) — i.e. Oracle has been absent from the public dollar market since February, which is the literal confirmation of JPM's "no return to the US debt market in CY26" (07-20) and makes the F1Q ATM disclosure the only funding event left in the calendar year. Semiconductors ran 5, 0, 0, 8, 0, 25, 0, 5 → **$42bn YTD, +57%**; **data-centre construction went 0 → $43bn**. Full-year 2026 estimates: **hyperscaler total $250–300bn; public IG total $350–400bn; total global credit markets $500–550bn.**
  - **MS's own view on the near term: *"we think issuance out of hyperscalers could slow in the near term, but we would fade a move back to the tights given 2027 capex estimates (~$1.4tr)."*** **The capex aggregate: MS's 2027 estimate is ~$1.4 TRILLION across six names — [[AMZN]], [[GOOG]], [[META]], [[MSFT]], ORCL and [[SPCX]] — and MS notes 2027 COMBINED capex estimates have NEARLY DOUBLED versus its November 2025 estimates, stating *"capex estimates now include SPCX."*** ⚠️ **NO PER-NAME SPLIT IS TAKEN FROM THIS NOTE: the exhibit is a column-interleaved chart whose per-name values cannot be read reliably from the PDF text layer. The aggregate and the constituent list are all that is on the page — do not back out an Oracle number from it.**
  - **🗓️ SINGLE-NAME WATCH FLAG: *"For Single Name, ORCL and AVGO remain front and center ahead of their respective September [reporting dates]."*** ➜ **Logged as a near-term catalyst on both pages. For Oracle that means the F1Q print (Sep 10) is now a CREDIT event as well as an equity one — the ATM disclosure lands into a market MS says it expects to keep widening.**
  - **Non-USD: only [[GOOG]] and [[AMZN]] have used non-dollar markets so far this year, so sector concentration is less pronounced outside USD; hyperscalers have shown EUR credit's ability to absorb QE-era-sized jumbo deals.** ⚠️ **Oracle is NOT among them — a channel two of its peers have opened and Oracle has not, which matters if the dollar market stays expensive.**
- **⚠️ A DEFINITIONAL RECONCILIATION WORTH ONE LINE BEFORE ANYONE NETS THESE NUMBERS (Goldman Sachs Credit Strategy Research · Amanda Lynam / Shamshad Ali / Sara Grut / Spencer Rogers, "Credit Notes: Expectations for Hyperscaler Debt Issuance: A Top-Down Approach", 2026-07-27).** GS's method starts from **GIR equity research's capex forecasts for FIVE hyperscalers — Amazon, Alphabet, Microsoft, Meta and ORACLE — FY2026 to FY2030**, plus actual FY2025 capex, matches them against actual and forecast **cash flow from operations** to assess sources vs uses, then **estimates a debt-financed share of hyperscaler capex**. **The number: *"So far in 2026 (as of July 25th) the hyperscalers have issued $194 billion of debt"* — EXCLUDING data-centre financings issued by special-purpose vehicles.** ⚠️ **GS's $194bn (as of July 25, five names including Oracle, ex-SPV data centres) and MS's $132bn "HQ Hyperscalers" + $25bn Oracle YTD through mid-August are NOT COMPARABLE WITHOUT RESTATING — GS's five include Oracle and exclude SPV data centres, while MS separates HQ hyperscalers, Oracle, SpaceX and data-centre construction into four buckets. Neither is a correction of the other, and the page should not present them as a revision.**
- **🔴🔴 THE CREDIT DESK THAT PUBLISHES THE ACTUAL BOND PRICES — AND ORACLE IS THE WIDEST-TRADING HYPERSCALER IN THE COMPLEX, BY A LARGE MARGIN, ON BOTH ITS OWN PAPER AND ITS OFFTAKE PAPER (Barclays FICC/Credit Research · Andrew Keches CFA / Jeff Harlib / Lea Overby / Elana Lipchak CFA / Remy Neuhaus, "AI Infrastructure Financing: Tracking Issuance Across Asset Classes: August 2026 Update", 2026-08-20).** A CREDIT note — no equity rating, PT or estimate — and it is the most directly useful source this section has had, because everything above is *models of* Oracle's funding need while this is *the price the market is charging for it*. **NOTHING ON THIS PAGE IS SUPERSEDED: Barclays corroborates the existing marks and adds precision.**
  - **① INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION OF THE SINGLE-DEAL FACT, FROM A SECOND DESK: Barclays' per-issuer table has ORCL at $25.0bn USD and $0 FOREIGN-CURRENCY issuance YTD 2026, from ONE deal dated 2/2/2026** (seven tranches priced 2/4: $3.0bn 4.55% '29, $3.5bn 4.95% '31, $3.0bn 5.35% '33, $5.0bn 5.70% '36, $2.25bn 6.55% '46, $5.0bn 6.70% '56, $2.75bn 6.85% '66 — $24.5bn ex-floaters). Against **[[AMZN]] $92.4bn ($62.0bn USD + $30.4bn foreign)**, **[[GOOG]] $80.3bn ($45.0bn + $35.3bn)**, **[[META]] $25bn (all USD)**, **[[SPCX]] $25bn (all USD)** and **[[MSFT]] $0**. Barclays' framing is blunt: *"AMZN/GOOGL have each issued more IG debt YTD than META/ORCL/SPCX combined"*, and AMZN/GOOGL "have come to market much more frequently via foreign currency transactions." ➜ **This is a second, independent verification of the MS Global Credit fact already on this page (Oracle's entire YTD was one February deal) AND of the negative that goes with it — Oracle has still not opened a non-dollar channel that two peers now fund ~35-44% of their IG needs through.** (Barclays, 2026-08-20)
  - **② 🔴 THE NUMBER THAT PUTS A DECIMAL POINT ON MS CREDIT'S "BOND YIELDS APPROACHING 8%" (08-14), FROM A DIFFERENT DESK SIX DAYS LATER: Barclays marks the February ORCL curve at YTWs of 5.38% ('29) / 5.91% ('31) / 6.38% ('33) / 6.75% ('36) / 7.64% ('46) / 7.75% ('56) / 7.90% ('66), at cash prices of 98.1 / 96.3 / 94.4 / 92.7 / 89.1 / 87.9 / 87.4.** So the long end is not *approaching* 8% — it is at 7.64-7.90%, and the paper is trading 11-13 points below par six months after issue. **Issue-to-current spread widening on Oracle's own tranches: '29 +95→+111 · '31 +115→+154 · '33 +130→+140 · '36 +145→+208 · '46 +170→+243 · '56 +180→+254 · '66 +195→+268 — i.e. +63 to +74bp of widening across everything 2036 and longer.** ⚠️ **FOR SCALE, AND THIS IS THE COMPARISON THAT MATTERS: on the same table, the equivalent long tranches widened +40bp for META ('66 +147→+187), +57bp for SPCX ('56 +175→+232), +27bp for GOOGL ('66 +95→+122) and +9bp for AMZN ('66 +118→+127). Oracle both STARTED the widest (issue spreads +95/+195 vs GOOGL's +27/+95) and WIDENED THE MOST. It is the widest-priced hyperscaler credit in the note on every tranche.** ⚠️ **DO NOT NET THIS AGAINST the "Hyperscalers incl. ORCL and SPCX +26bp YTD" bucket figure already on this page (MS, 08-14): that is a YTD index-bucket move, this is issue-to-current on named tranches. Different measures, not a revision.** (Barclays, 2026-08-20)
  - **③ 🔴 NET-NEW AND MATERIAL — THE OFF-BALANCE-SHEET CHANNEL, NAMED DEAL BY DEAL AND PRICED. The lease/purchase-obligation thread above (~$260bn not-yet-commenced, MS 06-22; ~$298bn incl. not-yet-commenced, DB 06-23) has never had the *bond* side attached to it. Barclays' AI-data-center bond table lists THREE deals whose tenant is ORACLE: **RDMICH** (Related Digital / RD Michigan Pro Owner I, 7.50% due 3/30/45, issued 4/24/26, **$14,000mn**, **974MW** critical IT load, **Baa3/NR/BBB — investment grade**, price 101.24, **YTW 6.99%, spread +326**) · **PFORGE** (Applied Digital / APLD ComputeCo 2 LLC, 6.75% '31, 3/3/26, **$2,150mn**, **200MW**, Ba3/NR/BB-, price 97.88, **YTW 7.36%, OAS +295**) · **YNDRDC** (Yondr Group / JK Land Holdings JV, Yondr JK 1 LLC, 6.875% '31, 6/23/26, **$715mn**, **48MW**, Ba3/NR/BB, price 93.50, **YTW 8.67%, OAS +426**).** **That is $16.87bn of third-party project bonds across 1,222MW of critical IT load funded against an Oracle lease** _(the $bn and MW totals are our sum of Barclays' Figure 7 rows, not a Barclays aggregate)_ — **equal to ~67% of Oracle's entire on-balance-sheet 2026 bond issuance, raised by other people's SPVs on the strength of Oracle's covenant.** **RDMICH at $14.0bn is the second-largest data-center bond in the whole table**, behind only Meta's $27.3bn Blue Owl JV. ➜ **This is what "funding relief, not resolution" looks like in the primary market: Oracle's capacity is being financed at real, observable prices in a channel that never touches its own balance sheet — which cuts BOTH ways. Constructive: the channel is open, deep and IG-capable. Bearish: it is Oracle's covenant being levered, so the obligation is economically Oracle's whether or not it is booked, which is precisely the WSJ/MS off-balance-sheet critique carried on the [[META]] page and in [themes/hyperscaler-capex.md](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md).** (Barclays, 2026-08-20)
  - **④ ⚠️ THE OFFTAKE-CREDIT DIFFERENTIAL IS NOW MARKET-PRICED, AND ORACLE'S LEASE IS THE EXPENSIVE ONE AMONG THE INVESTMENT-GRADE TENANTS.** On Barclays' same table: **Meta-tenanted paper trades at +200 (RPLDCI, NR/A+/NR) and +223 (TXEDCI, NR/A+/AA-); Microsoft-tenanted at +232 (QTSQST, Baa2) and +254 (QTSODY, Baa3/BBB-); Oracle-tenanted IG at +326 (RDMICH, Baa3/BBB)** — i.e. **the IG-rated Oracle-offtake bond prices roughly 100bp wide of the IG-rated Meta-offtake bonds and ~70-95bp wide of the Microsoft ones.** ➜ **Read against the on-page ratings row (ORCL Baa2/BBB-/BBB vs META and MSFT in the A-to-AAA range) this is the corporate credit gap showing up in the cost of *leased* capacity, not just in Oracle's own coupon — which is the same mechanism SemiAnalysis described from the landlord's side ("datacenter operators… can happily do 10-15-year offtakes with a hyperscaler like Microsoft directly") and MS credit described from the collateral side ("MS favours collateral-backed risk"; ORCL is the least collateral-backed of the large AI borrowers). ⚠️ **HEAVILY CONFOUNDED — DO NOT TREAT ~100bp AS A CLEAN ORACLE-vs-META CREDIT SPREAD: the deals differ in tenor (2045 vs 2048/2049), amortisation structure, sponsor, MW scale and rating agency coverage, and Barclays shows G-spread for IG and OAS for HY. The DIRECTION is solid and consistent with the ratings; the MAGNITUDE is not a like-for-like read.** (Barclays, 2026-08-20)
  - **⑤ THE MARKET ORACLE IS LEASING FROM IS ITSELF SCALING FAST, which is the constructive half: data-center bond issuance reached **~$76.2bn YTD 2026 ($33.6bn HY + $42.6bn IG)** versus **$35.9bn in full-year 2025 ($8.6bn HY + $27.3bn IG)**; two IG deals (TXEDCI, QTSODY) totalling $16.5bn and five HY deals (STNGRY, ELNFOR, YNDRDC, GALAXY, ZENARC) totalling $8.9bn have priced since Barclays' June update; total data-center bonds outstanding went from ~$27.3bn (Oct-25) to **$112.1bn (Aug-26 MTD)**; the market has "standardized" around **~5NC2 partial-amortising in HY vs construction-risk-mitigated fully-amortising in IG**; and Barclays tracks **~4.0GW of near-term HY pipeline** with "most developers… now repeat issuers." ➜ **The funding-availability question the bears price is being answered in the affirmative by the primary market — but at Oracle's spread, not at Meta's.** (Barclays, 2026-08-20)
  - **⑥ TWO ITEMS THAT SHARPEN THREADS ALREADY ON THIS PAGE RATHER THAN OPENING NEW ONES. (a) The Nvidia backstop, DATED AND SIZED: on **August 10** NVIDIA announced a **non-binding MoU with six investment firms — Apollo, BlackRock, Blackstone, Brookfield, Goldman Sachs and KKR — to establish separate financing platforms intended to mobilise more than $500bn of third-party capital over time for NVIDIA-powered AI infrastructure, with NVIDIA "may provide a residual-value support mechanism for up to 25% of an opportunity, assessed carefully on a project-by-project basis"** — the specific, dated version of the vendor-backstop thread this page carries from SemiAnalysis (07-06) and of the AMD-side backstop noted with it. **(b) The capex-mix framing: Barclays estimates HPC HARDWARE IS ~70% OF TOTAL AI DATA-CENTER CAPEX** and expects chip-financing facility issuance to accelerate as "a significant AI infrastructure financing vehicle during the next several years" — visible facilities to date **$68.6bn, of which $55.2bn in 2026** (vs $5.6bn in full-year 2025). ⚠️ **ORACLE IS NOT A PARTY TO ANY FACILITY IN THAT TABLE — the thirteen entries are neocloud DDTLs plus the AVGO/GOOGL JV. Logged as an absence, not a negative: Barclays flags "limited visibility to private market transactions," and Oracle's BYOH/prepay structure routes hardware financing through the *customer*, which is the on-page explanation for why it would not appear here.** (Barclays, 2026-08-20)
  - ⚠️⚠️ **THE GUARD-RAIL, AND IT EXTENDS THE MS-vs-GS ONE DIRECTLY ABOVE TO A THIRD PERIMETER: Barclays tracks ~$338bn of AI/digital-infrastructure financing YTD versus $179bn in FULL-YEAR 2025, +$118bn since its own first tracker on June 9** (the note's headline rounds this to "~$340bn"; the body figure is $338bn and is the one to quote, with both recorded). **That $338bn is USD-ONLY and spans IG ($224.6bn) + HY ($39.9bn) + securitised ($18.1bn) + visible HPC-hardware facilities ($55.2bn).** **It is NOT comparable to MS Global Credit's $445bn YTD / $217bn FY2025 ("across credit channels", 08-20) and NOT comparable to GS's $194bn as of July 25 (five names including Oracle, ex-SPV data centres, 07-27). THREE PERIMETERS, THREE NUMBERS, NONE A CORRECTION OF ANOTHER. Restate before comparing, and never present one as a revision of another.** ⚠️ **Also note the complex-level crowding marker, which is net-new to this page: hyperscalers are now **14% of ALL USD IG corporate supply YTD (~17% including data-center bonds)** and **21% of IG non-financial supply**, with **Tech (incl. META/AMZN/SPCX) at 37% — "a record for a single sector" if sustained** — and Barclays expects IG hyperscaler supply to KEEP GROWING ("the mix is broadening to include equity, but capex is also accelerating"), forecasting **~$285bn by year-end** against ~$248bn YTD across currencies. **Whatever Oracle discloses on the ATM at F1Q lands into that technical.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Barclays_AI_Infrastructure_Financing_Tracking_Issuance_Across_Asset_Classes_August_2026_Update.html)

- 🔴🔴 **THE CREDIT UPGRADE, NOW DATED AND SOURCED TO THE NOTE THAT MADE IT — J.P. MORGAN'S IG CREDIT DESK TOOK ORACLE **NEUTRAL → OVERWEIGHT ON 2026-07-07**, NOT ON 07-20, AND THE REASONING IS A PURE RELATIVE-VALUE CALL WITH A STATED WAITING PERIOD** (J.P. Morgan North America CREDIT Research · **Erica R. Spear** / Quinn Degen, "The Compute Must Flow: Project Finance 101 & Hyperscaler Ratings Refresh", **2026-07-07**, 41pp). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260707_JP_Morgan_ORCL_The_Compute_Must_Flow-_Project_Finance_101_-_Hyperscaler.html)
  ⚠️⚠️ **TWO FLAGS BEFORE THIS IS QUOTED ANYWHERE. (1) THIS IS A **CREDIT** RATING ON ORACLE'S BONDS — Overweight / Neutral / Underweight over a three-month horizon versus the relevant index or sector. **THERE IS NO EQUITY RATING AND NO PRICE TARGET IN THIS NOTE.** Oracle's EQUITY marks on this page (UBS Buy PT $245, DB Buy $300, JPM equity OW via Chatterjee, Redburn Sell $110) are unaffected and unchanged. (2) **~2 MONTHS STALE** — source 2026-07-07, folded 2026-09-01; every spread level in it is a July mark and is dominated by the 08-14 / 08-20 credit blocks below.**
  - **THE UPGRADE, IN THE DESK'S OWN WORDS: *"Oracle remains the widest-trading hyperscaler in our coverage, capacity continues to come online, and the bring-your-own-chip and chip prepayment strategy appears to be improving the funding profile. From a relative value perspective, Oracle stands out as the ONLY hyperscaler offering a genuinely attractive entry point at current spreads."*** ➤ **Note what the desk credits: BYOC + chip prepayments — the same two mechanisms this page tracks as the BYOH/prepay swing factor in the August triangulation above, and as the $4.6bn of disclosed customer prepayments MS called economically debt (08-25). A credit desk reading them as a funding-profile IMPROVEMENT is the constructive counterweight to that.**
  - ⚠️ **AND THE HEDGE, WHICH IS AS LOAD-BEARING AS THE UPGRADE: *"we view this as a LONGER-TERM CALL rather than one we expect to be rewarded immediately… the trade may take some time to work, however, as the company still carries a LARGE DEBT BURDEN, much of the NATURAL INVESTOR BASE IS ALREADY WELL-ALLOCATED, and the story has not yet improved enough to attract a meaningful wave of NEW BUYERS, particularly with prospective supply elsewhere across the hyperscaler group."*** ➤ **"The natural investor base is already well-allocated" is a technical observation this page has not had from any desk: the marginal buyer of Oracle paper is exhausted, independent of the fundamentals. It is the credit analogue of the equity-side ATM/dilution overhang.**
  - **THE THREE NAMED RISKS TO THE CREDIT CALL, all testable at the F1Q print: *"an UPWARD REVISION TO ITS CAPITAL PLAN that further pressures the funding profile, a SHIFT AWAY FROM EQUITY FUNDING, or a BROADER WIDENING IN HYPERSCALER SPREADS, any of which could keep the name rangebound or wider despite continued execution."*** ➤ **The middle one is the sharpest cross-check on this page: the credit desk wants the ~$20bn ATM equity leg to HAPPEN. The equity side treats the same ATM as dilution overhang. Same instrument, opposite sign — hold both.**
  - **THE SPREAD MARK, WITH ITS BASIS ATTACHED AND ITS DATE FLAGGED: Oracle is shown OFF-CHART at **+227bp** in JPM's Technology Benchmark 10s30s G-spread exhibit (Figure 2, spreads as of **6 July 2026**; cash prices adjusted 0.17bp per cash point), alongside CNXC (+473bp) and KD (+282bp) — i.e. Oracle sat outside the visible range of the entire IG technology benchmark.** ⚠️ **JULY MARK. Do NOT net it against the Barclays YTW ladder (5.38% '29 → 7.64% '46, 08-20) or the MS "approaching 8%" framing (08-14) — different metrics, different dates.**
  - **THE PEER SET, FOR CONTEXT ON THE SAME DAY: **[[MSFT]] credit DOWNGRADED to Underweight** (the only hyperscaler in JPM's credit coverage yet to access the bond market, against ~$190bn of calendar-2026 capex); **[[META]] credit DOWNGRADED to Neutral** (funding needs over eighteen months *"with no defined funding strategy"*); **[[GOOG]] and [[AMZN]] unchanged at Overweight.** ➤ **The inversion worth naming: the same desk was UNDERWEIGHT the hyperscaler with the most cash and OVERWEIGHT the one with the most debt, because in credit the variable is PROSPECTIVE SUPPLY. Oracle's ~$25bn of YTD issuance being DONE (one February deal, double-sourced below) is precisely why it screened attractive.**
  - **THE MECHANICS BEHIND THE FUNDING CHANNEL ORACLE IS USING — the note's 30-page "Project Finance 101" primer — IS FILED ON [hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md) rather than duplicated here.** The two items that bear most directly on Oracle: (a) **WAL, not stated maturity, is the right comparison** for amortising project bonds, so the offtake-paper spread differentials in ④ below must be compared at matched WAL; and (b) **termination for CAUSE — typically a prolonged failure to deliver contracted POWER or cooling — does NOT require the tenant to repay the debt**, whereas termination for convenience does. For a tenant whose sites depend on behind-the-meter gas (VoltaGrid/Jenbacher, 2.3GW), that asymmetry is the one to watch.
## Datacenter leasing & power footprint
- **Leasing leadership:** in 3Q25 Oracle led all hyperscalers, adding **+3,200 MW of new (non-commenced) leases**, vs ~600 MW MSFT / +1,000 MW GOOGL / +300 MW META; ORCL projected to ~5.4 GW of under-construction capacity, expansion explicitly tied to OpenAI agreements. Jefferies attributes the ~5x QoQ surge in industry leasing largely to "a single hyperscaler securing capacity for a major AI tenant, likely Oracle supporting OpenAI" (Jefferies 3Q25 Data Center Leasing). Aggregate non-commenced hyperscaler backlog ~15 GW across MSFT/ORCL/META/GOOGL.
- **Stargate site map (Barclays Powering AI):** Stargate 1 / Abilene TX (1.2–1.6 GW, Crusoe/Lancium, fully contracted by ORCL; ~$13mm/MW ex-IT); Frontier / Shackelford County TX (1.4 GW, Vantage, ~$18mm/MW ex-IT, 210 Jenbacher gas engines); Lighthouse / Port Washington WI (1 GW, Vantage, ~$17mm/MW ex-IT, WEC We Energies dedicated rate); Project Jupiter / Doña Ana NM (1.5 GW). July OpenAI/Oracle agreement to develop up to 4.5 GW of additional Stargate capacity under the $300B/5yr deal (a 600-MW Stargate-1 expansion + ~4 GW across three new sites). Full Stargate pipeline ~7 GW / >$400B planned (~$57mm/MW incl. IT).
- **Energy-as-a-service:** VoltaGrid to deploy **2.3 GW of power infrastructure for Oracle data centers** (INNIO Jenbacher engines + ABB), under a long-term, primarily fixed-price PPA — evidence distributed/behind-the-meter power is a structural, not bridge, solution for Oracle's grid-constrained sites (Barclays Powering AI).

Watch: any change to the 2028 launch framing, and whether STACK / Blue Owl disclose a response.

Management deferred BOTH the **gross-margin trajectory** and further **capex/margin detail** to it (FQ1 FY27 call, 2026-09-10), and **MS · Sanjit Singh has made GM-stabilisation evidence an explicit condition for moving off Equal-weight ($210)** — so this event carries real content, not reiteration. **Desk question set already on record (DB · Zelnick, MS · Singh, JPM · Chatterjee, 2026-09-11): (1) OCI build-out + growth strategy; (2) whether prepay / BYOHW / capital-light structures genuinely ease the financing constraint; (3) enterprise incumbency → AI Data Platform adoption; (4) AI packaging/pricing traction in Apps; (5) coding agents unlocking DB + legacy-app migrations.** ➤ **The falsifiable test carried over from the 09-11 consensus row: FY28 consensus moved only +0.58% against +$26bn of new RPO — if conversion is underwritten at this meeting, FY28 has to move by materially more.**

GS · Borges names it as one of the three open FY28 questions alongside **FY28 capex magnitude** and **future funding requirements** (2026-09-13). ➤ **Watch for a first dated FCF crossover at the 10-28 analyst meeting; its absence there would itself be the datapoint.**
