DB expects deliveries **"approaching 1GW" in F1Q vs 400MW+ in EACH of the prior two quarters** — a ~2.5x sequential step-up, with *"little spot or near-term capacity left to sell"* and **linearity through the quarter a swing factor DB has limited view into.** This is the physical number under the **+115% y/y @cc OCI** bogey (DB/Street; = Jefferies' 115%, ≈ BofA's IaaS +116%).**
  - **(c) 🔴 NON-GAAP GROSS MARGIN AT ~61% (−7+ pts y/y), and whether the OPERATING line holds at only −1pt y/y.** ➤ **Two different lines — do not score them as one. DB's thesis is that opex leverage plus recent cost actions absorb the AI-infra gross-margin hit, with FY27 OpEx dollars guided down slightly and NGOI growth accelerating to the mid-20s from +16% in FY26. Jefferies' 41% / MS's 40.9% operating-margin bogeys are the cross-check on the second line only.**
  - **(d) ⚠️ SaaS AND CLOUD DB INFLECTION — an uptick in SaaS growth after multiple quarters of faster deferred-backlog build (Jefferies models SaaS +11% y/y), ramping VA site go-lives, and a margin-rich Cloud DB inflection now that multicloud DB has GLOBAL REGIONAL AVAILABILITY across the three large CSPs, against management's **~40% y/y FY27 Cloud DB guide (+9pts acceleration, set at last year's FAM)**.**
- **🆕🔴🔴 F1Q27 PRINT — JEFFERIES PUBLISHED THE BOGEY GRID: "Key bogeys: 115% OCI growth, 41% opm, 40% RPO growth"** (Brent Thill, 2026-09-01/02). The full setup to score against: **Street total revenue FLAT q/q** (vs −6% / −7% declines in F1Q26 / F1Q25, i.e. *"notably LESS CONSERVATIVE"*); **OCI +115% y/y, a 22pt sequential ACCELERATION on a 3pt TOUGHER comp (52% → 55%)**; **SaaS +11% y/y**; **operating margin 40.9%, which would be ORCL's LOWEST IN 12 QUARTERS on GPU infra margin pressure**; **RPO $639bn (+40% y/y)** as ORCL laps the flagship AI contract.
- **🆕🔴 THE FOUR OVERHANGS TO TRACK, EACH WITH A NUMBER ATTACHED (Jefferies, 2026-09-02):** **① financing** — FCF −$24bn in FY26, **not positive until FY30E**, net leverage **~4.5x**; **② ~$260bn of off-balance-sheet DC commitments, nearly 3x FY27E capex of $90-95bn**; **③ Stargate execution** — reported site delays on **labour, permitting and power**; **④ ~47% of backlog tied to a SINGLE AI lab on a $300bn contract.** ➜ **Each is separately checkable at the print.**
- **🆕 THE WARRANT-LINKED [[HPE]] GIGAWATT-SCALE NETWORKING AGREEMENT (2026-09-02) IS UNSIZED AND ITS TERMS ARE UNDISCLOSED.** ➜ **Watch the 10-Q for the warrant terms Oracle received; this is the first time Oracle is on the RECEIVING end of the equity-linked structure the AI complex has been using.**
- **Q1 FY27 print: September 10, 2026** (AMC) — first look at the H2-weighted acceleration; watch cloud +58–64% guide delivery and capacity-online cadence.
- **Oracle Investor Day: October 28, 2026, Las Vegas** (AI World) — reaffirmation/update of FY30 +31% rev / +28% EPS CAGRs; new CFO Maxson's first framing of ROIC and funding.
- **🗓️🔴 Project Jupiter air-quality permit — formal public hearing scheduled for OCTOBER 19, 2026** (Oracle and its partners have filed to move it forward). The one *dated, binary* item on the New Mexico bear: UBS's channel check puts a **90% probability on the core 1.2 GW proceeding** and says the push-back "could scuttle the UP-SIZING of the site, but not the core 1.2 GW," with **200 MW of off-grid power already secured** (enough to light 2 of the 4 halls) protecting the 1H27 go-live and the full 1.2 GW landing "to the end of 2028." Watch also the **500 MW solar RFP** filed as a back-up plan (UBS · Keirstead, 2026-08-05).
- **F1Q FY27 disclosure is the named checkpoint for the ATM.** DB: the program was described only as "in progress" in management meetings and "**F1Q earnings disclosure is likely the next opportunity for a more specific update**" (DB · Zelnick, 2026-08-07); UBS has "heard from sources that Oracle's ATM issuance **has begun**" and assumes the $20bn completes by CY26 year-end, implying "**~$20bn of additional debt or equity in the period Jan-May 2027**" (UBS, 2026-08-05). JPM models FY27's $40bn as **50:50 debt/equity** (2026-08-13) — so the size and mix disclosed at F1Q is a direct test of three published models at once.
- **🗓️🔴 THE SEPTEMBER PRINT IS NOW A NAMED CREDIT-DESK EVENT, NOT ONLY AN EQUITY ONE: *"For Single Name, ORCL and [[AVGO]] remain front and center ahead of their respective September [reporting dates]"*** (Morgan Stanley Global Credit Strategy, "AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider", 2026-08-20). ⚠️ **Two things make this sharper than a normal preview: Oracle's ENTIRE YTD dollar issuance was a single $25bn February deal, so there is no recent pricing reference for whatever it does next; and MS is explicitly guiding to "a busy fall and continued spread widening." The ATM update therefore lands into a widening tape, which is the specific condition under which the "~22% YTD stock decline reduces the attractiveness of future equity raises" argument (MS credit, 2026-08-14) binds hardest.**
- **AMD Helios as an OCI capacity input — a supplier-side identification, not an Oracle disclosure.** A J.P. Morgan Asia hardware/supply-chain call names **Oracle and [[META]] as "the two major projects"** for **[[AMD]]'s Helios rack**, both in progress, with **mass production "could be around Q1 next year" (1Q27), "slightly after Nvidia"**, module-level volume possible in 2H26 and **rack-level shipment "more like first half next year"** (JPM Asia hardware/supply-chain call, Taiwan analyst rendered "Ivor", 2026-08-20). ➜ **Read against JPM equity's "first-of-its-kind 50,000-GPU AMD MI450 deployment" (08-13) and the standing chip-neutrality posture: this is the delivery schedule underneath that deployment, and it says the AMD leg of OCI's silicon diversification is a 1H27 rack event, not a 2H26 one.** ⚠️ **Source caveat, and it matters here: the transcript is a severe ASR defect (13,071 `[silence]` filler lines around ~20KB of content, heavy homophone garbling), so treat the NAMES as unreliable and the DATES as the usable part. Nothing in Oracle's own disclosure confirms it.**
- Capacity ramps at the five flagship sites (Abilene remainder; Shackelford/Doña Ana/Saline/Port Washington deliveries through 1H–2H CY27).
- Funding execution: ~$40B FY27 raise incl. $20B ATM equity; first private-credit / structured or hybrid issuance as a tell on balance-sheet flexibility; investment-grade rating preservation.
- Component-cost / memory-price trajectory vs. the pass-through contract mechanics; BYOH/prepaid mix shift (only ~12% of RPO is non-BYOH/prepaid per Materne/Evercore Q&A).
- OpenAI signals: whether OpenAI self-builds (relegating OCI) and the cadence at which the legacy Microsoft→Oracle sub-contract winds down vs. the direct OpenAI deal winding up.

## Risks
- **Customer concentration — OpenAI:** Barclays models OpenAI at **~76% of OCI's '28 AI capacity** and $300B/5yr; gives OpenAI material pricing leverage over Oracle and ties OCI's ramp to a still-unprofitable, privately-funded counterparty (UBS OpenAI deep-dive; Barclays on OpenAI). 4 customers each >$8B in Q4 FY26 (Magouyrk).
- **Capex intensity / cash burn** — reported capex well above the ~$70B net outlay; FCF –$9B/–$14B/–$20B FY26-28E; capex intensity to ~61% FY27E; funding via debt+equity + ATM dilution; capex-to-sales at dotcom-peak levels (MS, 2026-06-09; JPM AI Capex & Financing, 2025-11-10).
- **Credit / leverage** — most-indebted IG tech issuer (>$100B debt), mid-BBB with Negative outlooks; growing reliance on private/structured credit and hybrids; spread-widening risk (JPM, 2025-11-10).
- **Margin compression / low AI ROIC** through the build phase before contracted revenue ramps; gross margin down ~5pts FY26, further step-down guided FY27 (Maxson); third-party AI ROIC ~20% vs historical cloud profile (SemiAnalysis).
- **Component/supply-chain cost inflation** (memory/SSD/HDD) — mitigated by fixed-price-only-when-costs-known + pass-through clauses, but a live concern (Magouyrk, Q4 FY26).
- **Competition & overcapacity** — Neoclouds, hyperscaler self-build, OpenAI in-housing infra, new entrants pressuring price/share (Q4 FY26 Q&A; UBS OpenAI deep-dive).
- **Power/grid, construction labor and data-center delays** — industry-wide gating constraints; partial mitigation via VoltaGrid/Jenbacher EaaS (Jefferies/GEV power call, 2026-05-08; Barclays Powering AI).
- **FX** — large international mix; ~$2.4B earnings sensitivity to a 10% FX move (10-K FY25, Item 7A).

**THE COVERAGE AND THE CALLS:** **BUY** — Digital Realty (+25%), Equinix (+24%), Iron Mountain (+18%), Z.ai (+32%). **NEUTRAL** — [[AMZN]], Applied Digital, Cipher Digital, Core Scientific, Hut 8, IREN, [[MSFT]], MiniMax, TeraWulf. **SELL** — **[[NBIS]] (−60%, PT $84)**, **[[CRWV]] (−40%, PT $54)**, **[[ORCL]] (−33%)**.

### 🔴🔴🔴 (A) THE SELL, AND WHY THE STATED REASON MATTERS MORE THAN THE RATING

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE CALL, VERBATIM: *"**Oracle (SELL, −33%): lease-cost inflation, huge lease commitments for assets that [have] not yet commenced, declining ROI.**"*** ➤ **Every clause is an ACCOUNTING claim, not a demand claim. Redburn is not arguing Oracle's backlog is fake — it is arguing that the obligations Oracle has taken on to serve it are understated in reported cash flow. That is the same construct behind the note's headline sector finding: hyperscaler reported FCF of **+$126bn** (June-2026 TTM) becomes **−$151bn** *"on an 'economic' basis once you deduct the change in implied capital from UNCOMMENCED LEASES, a swing of nearly $280bn."*** ⚠️ **That adjustment is Redburn's own non-GAAP construct — defensible as a way to see through operating-lease treatment, not a reported number.**
- AND THE ONE THAT IS TESTABLE ON THIS PAGE SOONEST: *"Around **70% of the incremental hyperscaler backlog** now comes from major agreements with [[OPENAI]], [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[META]], so **either future contracts catch up or MATERIAL CAPACITY IS BEING BUILT AHEAD OF COMMITTED DEMAND.**"* ➤ **Check against the next RPO disclosure.**

### 🔴🔴 (B) PROJECT JUPITER — THE PRICED SIGNAL NOW HAS A STRUCTURE AND A SIZE

- The **$18bn of loans tied to the New Mexico data centre leased to Oracle slid into stressed territory on Friday, privately quoted at 89 to 91 cents on the dollar**, on investor wariness about construction delays amid local pushback (FT, via Jefferies · Favuzza, MS TMT desk · Wigg and UBS TECH · Ruple, all 2026-09-21). ✅ **Already logged 09-20; what is NET-NEW today is the price (89-91) and the structure below.**
- ⚠️⚠️ THE STRUCTURE, FROM A LOW-QUALITY SOURCE AND FLAGGED AS SUCH: @zerohedge describes *"a 'simplified' structure of **Oracle's Project Jupiter SPV (4 data centers on 1400 acres in NM)** providing **$300BN in compute to OpenAI** and funded by **Bonds, Loans and Blue Owl equity**"* (2026-09-21). ⚠️⚠️⚠️ **THE SIZE ($300bn), THE SITE COUNT, THE ACREAGE AND THE BLUE OWL EQUITY LEG ARE UNVERIFIED AT THIS SOURCE, and the post's closing claim — *"The project is now HOPELESSLY MOTHBALLED and may never see completion"* — is EDITORIAL, carries no sourcing, and CONTRADICTS the FT's own framing of construction DELAYS. NOT ADOPTED. It is logged only because the SPV's existence and its OpenAI counterparty are the two facts that make the 89-91 quote interpretable, and because it names a structure the wiki should try to verify.**
- ✅ AND A SECOND, INDEPENDENT CREDIT DATAPOINT IN THE SAME 24 HOURS, HEADLINE ONLY: *"Jane Street-Linked Data Center Debt Sours"* (The Information · Dakin Campbell, 2026-09-21 — ⚠️ **PAYWALLED, headline only, no figures on disk**). ➤ **Two distinct data-center credits reported as souring in one news cycle. Neither is Oracle-specific and neither should be treated as a trend on its own — but the CLASS of signal is now recurring, and this page should track it as a series.**
- **CONTEXT FROM THE SAME FT/desk cycle: *"Big Tech has added **up to ~$300B of RESIDUAL VALUE GUARANTEES (RVGs) in <12 months** to finance AI data centers and chips **while keeping most of the associated debt OFF its own balance sheets**"* — already on file, and the exact mechanism Redburn's "economic FCF" adjustment is designed to capture.**

⚠️⚠️⚠️ **FOUR GUARDS BEFORE ANY OF THIS IS USED:**
- **(a) THE $45bn/GW IS A *COMPUTE PRICE* BASIS AND IS THE FIFTH INCOMPATIBLE $/GW NUMBER NOW CIRCULATING ON THIS WIKI.** It is NOT [[NVDA]] management's *"DC opportunity progressing to $40B/GW"* (Bernstein, same day), NOT Hock Tan's ~$30bn ARR/GW, NOT Jefferies' $11.5→$15bn/GW of [[AVGO]] CONTENT per GW, and NOT the $40bn/GW in the Wells Fargo META arithmetic. **NEVER NET OR CHAIN THEM.**
- **(b) THE ~700 MW CLAIM IS THE LOAD-BEARING ONE AND IT IS THE MOST CONTESTABLE.** It is derived from **OPEN-WEIGHT** models (Kimi K3, GLM-5.3, DeepSeek V4), not from the frontier closed models the buildout is actually sized for, and *"tuned to real world usage"* is doing unspecified work. If inference genuinely needs an order of magnitude less capacity than the market assumes, most of this note follows; if the open-weight proxy understates frontier inference cost, none of it does. **This is the single number to attack or defend.**
- **(c) THE ④ ROUND-TRIPPING ARITHMETIC IS AN INFERENCE, NOT AN IDENTITY.** The 35% margin is Redburn's assumption; a multi-year contract's gross profit and a point-in-time equity stake are not commensurable without a term structure. The 70%-of-incremental-backlog figure IS testable against the next round of 10-Q RPO disclosures.
- **(d) THE "ECONOMIC FCF" ADJUSTMENT IN ① IS A NON-GAAP CONSTRUCT OF REDBURN'S OWN DESIGN** — deducting the change in implied capital from leases that have not commenced. It is a defensible way to see through operating-lease treatment, and it is not a reported number.

| **08-31** | 🔴🔴 **Deutsche Bank · Brad Zelnick / Bhavin Shah — "US Software: Highlights from the 2026 DB Tech Conference" (fireside + meetings with Co-CEO Mike Sicilia, CFO Hilary Maxson, SVP IR Ken Bond)** — **MANAGEMENT VOICE hosted by a broker: no rating, no PT, no estimates in this section** | demanda / produto / capital | **bull** | ★ **Three items that bear directly on the standing debates on this page: the New Mexico derisking, the funding message, and the Cloud Database inflection.** 🔴 **CLOUD INFRASTRUCTURE — THE DERISKING ITEM IS NAMED AND NARROWED: construction at the large-scale sites supporting *"a majority of RPO backlog"* continues, **consistent with FY27 guidance reiterated last quarter.** *"This includes NEW MEXICO, where their partner's recent GAS PIPELINE UPDATE was framed as VALIDATING THE MULTIPLE VIABLE OPTIONS available and A PENDING AIR PERMIT AS THE ONE MAJOR DERISKING ITEM LEFT."* ⚠️ **That is a single, dateable, binary gating item on the largest tranche of backlog — worth tracking as a discrete catalyst rather than as generic execution risk.** **Demand signals *"remain strong looking out MULTIPLE YEARS,"* and management argues its **presence through the application layer gives it a fuller view of the AI value chain than peers** and the ability to adjust faster if anything changes. **Funding messaging *"UNCHANGED from what we heard on our July NDR."*** 🔴 **CLOUD DATABASE — FY27 CALLED AN INFLECTION POINT: management points to the financial targets outlined at FAM, with optimism on AI-enablement tailwinds and the **ramp of Multicloud**. Oracle DB positioned as *"the BRIDGE BETWEEN MODELS AND ENTERPRISE CONTEXT required for unleashing the value of AI (i.e., inference)."* **Multicloud revenue +4x y/y in F4Q**, attributed primarily to the first two partnerships (Azure and Google); **global regional availability on AWS reached more recently — 22 regions as of F4Q — and is expected to provide a more tangible boost in FY27.*** 🔴 **THE FORM-FACTOR ARGUMENT, which is a genuinely differentiated claim in a power-constrained market: *"differentiated form factors broaden this opportunity, enabling the FULL ORACLE CLOUD (OCI + database + Apps) to be deployed in FEWER THAN A DOZEN RACKS within existing enterprise or sovereign datacenters… all the more compelling in today's POWERED SHELL CONSTRAINED environment."*** ⚠️ **APPLICATIONS — THE HONEST BIT: management sees *"NO EVIDENCE AI IS REPLACING SaaS APPS,"* but concedes AI *"has contributed to SOME ELONGATION IN SALES CYCLES"* — characterised as **concentrated in SMB (e.g. NetSuite), with NO noticeable slowdown in Enterprise or Government.** Accelerating deferred revenue growth attributed to Enterprise/Government strength and rising win rates tied to AI embedded in Fusion Apps (14k Fusion Apps customers cited). **Multiple AI monetization models — token bundles, usage-based and outcome-based pricing — expected to COEXIST.*** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260831_Deutsche_Bank_NOW_US_Software-_Highlights_from_the_2026_DB_Tech_Conference.html) |
| **08-31** | 🔴🔴 **UBS · Carl Keirstead — "State of AI and Software" call: the $10bn/GW anchor Oracle set is now being repriced by the market, UPWARD** | valuation / demanda | **bull** | ★ **The single most useful cross-name datapoint of the call, and Oracle is the reference point for it.** 🔴 **UBS on revenue per gigawatt, *"that we should ALL BE USING to build our hyperscaler revenue estimates"*: *"Obviously, ORACLE PUT THEIR $10 BILLION PER GIGAWATT NUMBER IN OUR HEADS LAST FALL. More recently, SPACEX AND [[NBIS]] HAVE SAID THAT THEY'RE SIGNING NEW DEALS AT A $30 TO $50 BILLION PER GIGAWATT LEVEL."*** ⚠️ **UBS ATTACHES TWO CATCHES AND THEY MUST TRAVEL WITH THE NUMBER — this is exactly the kind of figure that gets chained into a series and shouldn't be: (1) *"these are for [[NVDA]] VERA RUBIN GPU CLUSTERS… we need to be more thoughtful about how this pending GPU mix, H100s to Blackwells to Vera Rubins, are going to CASCADE into hyperscaler growth rates"*; and (2) *"these are SHORT-TERM, SIX-MONTH DEALS such that they get priced AT CLOSER TO SPOT RATES RATHER THAN LONG-TERM CONTRACTED RATES."* SpaceX is doing six-month deals for flexibility (optionality to allocate 2027 capacity to Grok); Nebius *"appears to be doing this for PURE ASP LIFT."* [[MSFT]] told UBS it *"will NOT chase short-term deals."*** 🔴 **UBS's conclusion, which is the actionable part for this page: *"I want to be more thoughtful about WHERE ON THAT REVENUE PER GIGAWATT SPECTRUM, $10 BILLION TO $30 BILLION, THE HYPERSCALARS ARE MOVING TO as it could obviously have A PROFOUND EFFECT ON OUR AZURE, AWS, AND ORACLE OCI ESTIMATES. THOSE FIGURES SHOULD BE BIASED UP GIVEN GPU PRICING ALONE."* An explicit upward bias to OCI revenue estimates, driven by pricing rather than volume. Cross-file to the rev-per-GW ledger.** **Positioning: Oracle named among UBS's positively-biased ideas — *"on the right side of the AI trend… not already crowded longs with hefty multiples and hefty expectations… Still debated, multiples can be defended, growth rates are accelerating"* — alongside [[MSFT]] and [[PLTR]].** ⚠️ **UBS also flags competition as an open question, not a settled one: *"we now have SpaceX and [[META]] selling spare capacity… [[ANTHROPIC]] striking a massive $45 BILLION DEAL WITH NSCALE. So, we need to just dig further. Is this a natural reflection of an industry facing a wall of demand with plenty to go around OR DO WE NEED TO BE WORRIED ABOUT COMPETITION, PRICING, AND RETURNS DOWN THE ROAD?"*** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_31_ubs_the_state_of_ai_software.html) |

| **08-13** | 🔴🔴 **JPM · Samik Chatterjee — "Re-rating and Rapid Scaling Drive Upside Opportunity Despite Robust Capital Needs". OVERWEIGHT reiterated, Dec-27 PT $200 (prior Dec-26 PT $210); px 08-12 $153.28** | **demanda / capital / valuation** | **bull (with the capital caveat stated up front)** | 🔴 **THE NUMBER THAT DEFINES THE CALL: "Oracle's rapid build-out of cloud infrastructure is expected to deliver OCI / IaaS revenue ramp from $18 bn in FY26 to $180 BN IN FY30 (per JPMe)" — a 10x in four years — "which will put Oracle firmly in the competitive landscape with hyperscalers including [[MSFT]], [[AMZN]], and [[GOOG]]."** Backstop: **$638bn of RPO as of last quarter, "which comprises contracts with [[OPENAI]] and [[META]], amongst others."** Model: **33% total-company revenue CAGR through FY30 and ~29% EPS CAGR**, with **IaaS gross margins of 30–40%** — so *"we expect the company to focus on maintaining OPERATING margins, while company GROSS margins moderate"* as IaaS mixes up. ⚠️ **THE CAPITAL LEG, AND JPM IS EXPLICIT THAT IT IS BIGGER THAN MANAGEMENT IMPLIES: "the build-out of the infrastructure is AHEAD OF MONETIZATION through revenues, and will, in our estimate, require the company to raise about ~$20 BN OF CAPITAL ON AVERAGE EVERY YEAR (inclusive of the $40 bn in FY27); although admittedly OUR CAPEX FORECASTS ARE HIGHER THAN THOSE IMPLIED BY THE MANAGEMENT TEAM."** The valuation argument is the discount itself: *"Concerns around the capital intensity… as well as **near-term anticipation of an equity issuance**, are leading ORCL shares to trade at a **significant discount to both peer groups of AI infra builders as well as Enterprise software companies**."* ⚠️ **Note the PT arithmetic: $210 (Dec-26) → $200 (Dec-27) is a LOWER absolute target on a LATER date — i.e. a de-rating of the valuation despite the reiterated Overweight. Logged as such rather than as a "PT cut" headline.** **★★ DEEPENED FROM THE FULL NOTE — the row previously carried the headline call without the model. All below net-new (JPM · Samik Chatterjee / Jaiden Patel / Arti Vula / Brian Hyska / Mashu Nishi, 2026-08-13):** **PT MATH: "Our Dec 2027 price target of $200 is based on ~13x EV/PRO FORMA OPERATING INCOME on our 2028 PF operating income forecast of $62 BN," explicitly "at a discount to peers" (peer sets = AI infra builders [[AMZN]]/[[GOOG]]/[[MSFT]]/[[META]]/EQIX/DOCN and enterprise software CRM/SAP/ADBE/NOW/WDAY). ORCL "trading at the LOWEST GROWTH-ADJUSTED MULTIPLE amongst the hyperscalers looking to build AI infrastructure, both on an EV/PF operating income as well as EV/GAAP operating income basis."** **ESTIMATES (FYE May): revenue $67,358m FY26A → $90,953m FY27E (+35.0%) → $131,488m FY28E (+44.6%); adj. EBIT $28,925m → $37,628m → $51,398m (margin 42.9% → 41.4% → 39.1%); adj. EPS $7.63 → $8.05 → $11.10 (vs BBG $7.48/$8.06/$10.71); GROSS MARGIN 65.8% FY26 → 58.9% FY27 → 51.0% FY28 — the mix-down quantified. **FY28 adj. EPS RAISED to $11.10 from $10.59 (+4.7%)** — the only estimate change in the note. CFO $31,977m → $59,884m → $91,759m against **FCFF of -$23,686m / -$35,116m / -$27,741m**. Net debt/EBITDA 3.3x → 2.6x → 2.2x; share count 2,914m → 3,103m → 3,167m (i.e. ~250m shares / ~8.7% dilution embedded by FY28).** 🔴 **THE CAPEX LADDER — JPM's is the highest on the page and it is explicit about being above the company: gross capex ~$9bn FY23 → ~$7bn FY24 → ~$21bn FY25 → ~$56bn FY26 → **~$95bn FY27E → ~$120bn FY28E → ~$133bn FY29E → ~$140bn FY30E**" (+70% FY27, then +26% / +11% / +6%), "materially above those implied by the company in their long-term outlook as we forecast the demand backdrop to lead the company to invest in growth in the out-years."** ⚠️ **Note the FY28 collision: JPM ~$120bn ≈ DB's ~$120bn gross-capex base case (08-07), against UBS's $109.7bn (08-05) and a Street consensus UBS puts at $92bn. Three houses this week all sit ABOVE the Street on FY28 capex.** **CONTEXT ON SCALE: "the ~$140 bn in capital investment forecasted for FY30 will screen as a PARADIGM SHIFT in capex for the company," yet still below JPMe 2030 capex for [[MSFT]] $380bn, [[GOOG]] $537bn, [[META]] $320bn — and ahead of [[CRWV]] at $94bn.** 🔴 **THE CAPITAL-RAISE LADDER behind the "~$20bn/yr average," which the page had only as an average: "$40 bn of capital raise in FY27 WITH A BALANCE OF EQUITY AND DEBT, and another $14 BN IN FY28, $20 BN IN FY29 and $7 BN IN FY30, which equates to an average of $20 bn each year through to FY30" — with the footnoted assumption that **FY27 is 50:50 DEBT vs EQUITY and FY28 onward is DEBT ONLY.** So JPM's model embeds ~$20bn of FY27 equity issuance and then no further equity — a testable, falsifiable structure to hold against actual issuance.** **BALANCE SHEET: "$129.5 BN of gross debt as of the last quarter end, up from $75 bn in FY22," 4Q26 net leverage **3.3x, the highest amongst the hyperscalers**; recent deals a $25bn bond offering in Feb-2026 plus ~$18bn in Sep-2025 (six tranches, 4.45-6.10%, out to 2065). Ratings: Moody's Baa2 outlook Negative; **S&P moved ORCL to BBB- on JULY 9, 2026** — "the lowest investment-grade rung and one notch above high-yield" — citing rising debt and ~$300bn OpenAI counterparty concentration; Fitch BBB/stable. Net debt forecast **$140bn end-CY27 and $171bn end-CY28.**** **SCALE VS THE BIG 3, which is the "why this can keep compounding" exhibit: OCI's **$18.1bn FY26 infrastructure revenue is "roughly ONE NINTH of AWS (~$169 bn run-rate)," below Azure (~$123 bn) and Google Cloud (~$99 bn) — "but it is growing far faster (+93%, vs. AWS +37%, Azure +43%, Google +82%) and HAS A BACKLOG LARGER THAN THAT OF GOOGLE AND AMAZON."** Recognised-revenue proof of the ramp: **OCI quarterly revenue $2.1bn F1Q25 → $5.8bn F4Q26, a 2.7x increase in under 24 months.** On capacity, ">1.2 GW delivered in FY26 (and expects to bring online another 1 GW in F1Q27) is most comparable to Microsoft's ~1 GW ADDED PER QUARTER." Gartner still has Oracle at "a modest SINGLE DIGIT PERCENTAGE of the IaaS market."** **THE INDUSTRY BACKDROP JPM underwrites the RPO with (from its own comms-infra/Asia-hardware teams): global DC capacity **60 GW (2025) → 253 GW (2031)** against demand **116 GW → 532 GW** — "a significant supply-demand gap even in the long term, leaving a tight capacity backdrop."** **RPO COMPOSITION, and it is more diversified than the page's OpenAI framing alone: the $638bn (+363% y/y) followed "a significant inflection sequentially of over $318 BN IN F1Q26," with contracts signed with "[[OPENAI]], xAI, [[META]], [[NVDA]], [[AMD]], and many others." Diversification evidence JPM cites: at the October FAD, **"$65 bn contracted within just 30 days across SEVEN DEALS AND FOUR CUSTOMERS, NONE OF WHICH WERE OPENAI"**; in F4Q26, four separate customers each >$8bn plus enterprise wins called out at **Vodafone and Claro**. Fungibility backstop: of 35k GPUs from 59 customers up for renewal in F4Q26, 49% of customers renewed 92% of the GPUs "with most of the remaining 8% subsequently SOLD TO OTHER CUSTOMERS WITHIN THE QUARTER."** ⚠️ **BUT JPM'S OWN CONCENTRATION ESTIMATE IS THE HIGHEST ON THE PAGE: in Risks — "we estimate OPENAI REPRESENTS ~50%+ OF ORACLE'S $638BN RPO, directly and via the Stargate project. Should the anchor tenant restructure, reduce, or be unable to pay… the headline RPO would OVERSTATE COLLECTIBLE REVENUE." Compare UBS eight days earlier at ~40% (~$250bn). Also logged: the $300bn/5yr OpenAI agreement is described as including "a delivered capacity of 4.5GW."** **On the circularity question JPM is dismissive but explicit: [[NVDA]] supporting OpenAI, OpenAI committing to Oracle, and "Oracle's $40 BN+ PURCHASE OF GPUs FROM NVIDIA" is "a further challenge to investor sentiment. However, we believe this is a SPECIFIC CUSTOMER INSTANCE and unlikely to become broad-based." Counterparty comfort: OpenAI's **March 2026 raise of $122bn** (investors incl. [[AMZN]], NVDA, SoftBank) and reported annualised revenue "on the order of **$20-25 bn, or roughly $2 bn / month, by spring 2026**."** 🔴 **THE MARGIN NUMBER THE BULLS DO NOT QUOTE, and it is in JPM's own Risks section: the FY30 framework requires "AI-cloud gross margins to CONVERGE FROM REPORTED MID-TEENS toward the 30-40% lifetime target as utilization, scale, and higher-margin mix arrive." REPORTED MID-TEENS — that is a materially worse starting point than the "32% in Q3 FY26" the page carries from management, and it is the single best piece of support for the Redburn (07-20) SELL thesis to appear in an OVERWEIGHT note.** **THE UNIT ECONOMICS FROM THE FAD, decomposed: ~$10bn of revenue per GW; land/data-centre/power costs "35% of total, or ~$2 bn/GW/year"; compute/networking/storage depreciation "$4.2 bn/GW/year"; a six-year depreciation cycle implying **"total depreciation-related expenses for IT equipment… to be $25 bn/GW"** — leaving room for 35% gross margins. ⚠️ **JPM's own caveat is a forward bear: $25bn/GW "appears MORE REPRESENTATIVE OF THE CURRENT GENERATION OF GPUs rather than the upcoming generations, WHICH WE EXPECT WILL BE MORE EXPENSIVE."** JPM frames the near-term compression as timing, not structure — expenses land on the P&L before revenue while shells are fitted and tested, so "the margin headwinds are TEMPORARY, RATHER THAN STRUCTURAL," with magnitude set by the number of concurrent projects.** **BYOH QUANTIFIED BY JPM: most of the $67bn added to AI-infra contracts last quarter were BYOH/BYOC or prepaid, taking the BYOH/BYOC book to $75bn of the $638bn RPO; on JPM's simplified model "gross margin percentage over the period of the contract will rise to **~48%**, although GROSS PROFIT DOLLARS REMAIN INTACT."** ➜ **Compare WFC's mgmt-sourced ~55% BYOH vs ~40% comparable (06-11) — same direction, different level; JPM's is a modelled contract, WFC's an illustrative mgmt example.** **OTHER MARGIN LEVERS JPM lists: better pricing off the tight supply backdrop "including CONTRACTING CAPACITY CLOSER TO DELIVERY of the data center" (the direct answer to the Jefferies 08-10 duration bear); multi-vendor chip/networking diversification incl. white box; and attach of AI database services with enterprise inference capacity.** **DIFFERENTIATION — third-party corroboration JPM leans on: "SemiAnalysis has independently credited OCI with roughly a **20% CAPEX ADVANTAGE VERSUS A LEADING NEOCLOUD**, achieved by building on standard RoCE-OVER-ETHERNET networking rather than costlier InfiniBand, sourcing FOXCONN ODM servers, and financing the build at INVESTMENT-GRADE as opposed to the rates of some neocloud peers." Plus the **Acceleron architecture and Zettascale10 cluster "engineered toward as many as 800,000 CONNECTED GPUs"** and **"a first-of-its-kind 50,000-GPU [[AMD]] MI450 deployment"** giving silicon optionality. Chip-neutrality is a stated posture — Oracle sold its Ampere stake and stays clear of custom silicon while AWS (Trainium), Google (TPU) and Microsoft (Maia) build their own.** **THE STRUCTURAL BULL, and it is the part of this note least represented elsewhere on the page: "the durable differentiation sits ABOVE BARE METAL and Oracle is ONE OF THE ONLY GPU-SCALE CLOUDS THAT ALSO OWNS THE ENTERPRISE SYSTEM OF RECORD" — AI Vector Search in Database 23ai/26ai (inference/RAG against data already in the production DB, no ETL), Exadata Exascale, Fusion/NetSuite attach. "The bare-metal business can prove to be the WEDGE or entry point… then spend the multi-year life of that contract driving attach via Oracle Database, autonomous data services, and the rest of the PaaS stack. Each layer it attaches is STICKIER AND HIGHER-MARGIN than the compute beneath it."** ⚠️ **This is the direct rebuttal to Jefferies' 08-10 "Oracle's OpenAI deal is very much for bare metal" attach-gap bear — and to UBS's unresolved concern #2 ("ability to move up the stack"). Both sides now on the page.** **DATABASE / APPS, with the numbers: multicloud DB live across "roughly 33 Azure regions, ~20 on AWS, and ~14-15 on Google," compounding **+1,529% / +817% / +531% / +404% y/y across the last four quarters** ("deceleration only attributable to a much larger base"); logos Air France-KLM, Activision, PepsiCo, Vodafone, Fidelity; **Ellison's floated aspiration of "~$20 bn of multicloud database revenue within five years."** Market context: the **~$160bn DBMS market growing ~18% in 2026 (Gartner)**, cloud DB services ~two-thirds of spend — but **Oracle "has slipped from category leader (through 2019) to ~#3 in DBMS revenue behind AWS and Microsoft."** Apps: Cloud Applications +11% y/y FY25→FY26; **Fusion ~$6bn and NetSuite ~$4.4bn annualised at F4Q26, together ~55-60% of SaaS**; Oracle Health (the ~$28.3bn Cerner deal) has "grown below double digits since," targeting a return to double-digit growth in FY27 on a new AI-native EHR with the VA rolling out in 2026.** 🔴 **THE HALF OF ORACLE NOBODY MODELS: "the NON-CLOUD SEGMENTS, including Software, Hardware and Services tracked to **$33.4 BN IN FY26, ABOUT ~50% OF REVENUE, AND BARELY GREW, EXPANDING AT 1% Y/Y**," flat on a three-year view ($33.2bn FY24 / $32.9bn FY25 / $33.4bn FY26). JPM argues stagnation ≠ disruption — highest switching costs in enterprise software (RAC, Exadata, converged engine), still the most popular database in the world per DB-Engines — and reframes it as **"the CASH GENERATION ENGINE FUNDING IN PART the capital-intensive infrastructure build-out."** ➜ **That is the cleanest statement on the page of why the funding debate is survivable, and it is the asset UBS independently flags as the thing anchoring the IG rating.** (JPM, 2026-08-13) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JP_Morgan_ORCL_Oracle_Re-rating_and_Rapid_Scaling_Drive_Upside_Opportu.html) |
| 08-10 | 🔴 **Jefferies · Max + Shane — post-2Q26 hyperscaler call (Capstone-hosted; PRIMARY transcript). READ-THROUGH: the cleanest structural statement yet of WHY ORACLE DOES NOT CAPTURE THE COMPUTE-PRICING SPIKE** | margem | **bear** | ★★ **THE MECHANISM, verbatim: gigawatt monetisation has gone from "8 TO 10 BILLION, MAYBE EVEN 12 BILLION per [gigawatt of] revenue" at the start of 2026 to "30 TO 50 BILLION PER GIGAWATT" now — "PRICING 3 TO 5X FOR A GIGAWATT IN THE SPAN OF SIX MONTHS." And then the divergence: "where there's a divergence that happens is IF YOU LOOK AT LIKE [[CRWV]] AND ORACLE, WHERE YOU'RE DOING A FIVE-YEAR CONTRACT WITH LOCKED-IN UNIT ECONOMICS — AND YOU DON'T HAVE MUCH TILT TO ANY OF WHAT'S HAPPENING ON ON-DEMAND AND SPOT INSTANCES. When you look at THE HYPERSCALERS, THEY OBVIOUSLY DO. But the nature of [Oracle's and CoreWeave's] business is that THEY NEED TO HAVE THESE LONG LOCKED-IN CONTRACTS TO ENSURE THAT THEY'RE ABLE TO DO THE RAISE BEHIND IT."** ➜ **This is the funding structure being named as the source of the economic disadvantage: the debt raise requires contract duration, and contract duration forecloses participation in a 3-5x spot repricing. It is the same RPO the bulls cite, read as a cap rather than as an asset.** ⚠️ **THE DEMAND-SIDE COROLLARY, and it applies directly to Oracle's model: "I DON'T KNOW IF ANY LAB WANTS TO MAKE A COMMITMENT FOR A FIVE-YEAR DEAL AT 30 TO 50 BILLION PER GIGAWATT" — the short-dated deals being struck now carry 90-day cancellation options, which is precisely why they price at a premium and why five-year paper cannot.** ⚠️ **AND THE ATTACH POINT, which is where Oracle is thinnest versus [[MSFT]]: on the [[OPENAI]] relationship — "Oracle, that deal is going to be VERY [MUCH] FOR BARE METAL. But OpenAI and Microsoft will leverage FIVE DIFFERENT OTHER DRIVERS — they'll be using COSMOS, AZURE BLOB, a lot of these STORAGE AND OTHER APPLICATION OFFERINGS in addition to bare-metal instances. This is why THE HYPERSCALERS HAVE MORE TORQUE on what you've seen from a pricing side."** ⚠️ **Read-through only — Jefferies' internet team does not cover Oracle here and gives no ORCL rating, PT or estimate; this is a structural argument, not a coverage view.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_10_max_jeffries_post_2q26.html) |
| 08-07 | 🔴 **Deutsche Bank · Brad Zelnick — "Flexing Funding Mix." Buy, PT $300 (ref $144.39). The clearest statement yet that the FUNDING question, not the business, is what the market is trading.** | capital | **mixed** | **★ THE FRAMING IS THE FINDING: "Following our time on the road in NY with Oracle management, we've spoken with DOZENS OF INVESTORS over the past two weeks; THE NUMBER ONE, TWO AND THREE QUESTIONS IN NEARLY EVERY CONVERSATION ARE AROUND HOW AND ON WHAT SCHEDULE ORACLE PLANS TO FUND THE BUILDOUT of its cloud infrastructure to deliver against a ~$600BN BACKLOG of committed customer contracts."** **The sequence DB lays out, which is the most useful part because it dates each decision point: (1) the previously announced ATM PROGRAM, characterised only as "IN PROGRESS" during the meetings — "F1Q EARNINGS DISCLOSURE IS LIKELY THE NEXT OPPORTUNITY FOR A MORE SPECIFIC UPDATE." (2) "The NEXT $20BN IN FUNDING the company expects to raise as part of its announced FY27 CAPITAL PLAN. THE TOP PRIORITY HERE IS MAINTAINING AN IG CREDIT RATING; mgmt indicated that POST-ATM THEY STILL EXPECT TO HAVE ACCESS TO IG MARKETS BUT WE COULDN'T HELP WALKING AWAY FEELING LIKE EQUITY COULD BE A MEANINGFUL PART OF THE MIX AS WELL," consistent with prior commentary on a "combination of debt and equity." (3) FY28, where "THE POSSIBLE RANGE OF OUTCOMES IS WIDER" given "A LACK OF SPECIFICS OFFERED ON FY28 OCF, CAPEX OR CAPITAL PLANS TO DATE," against guidance for capex to "PEAK" over the FY27/28 period.** 🔴 **DB'S OWN FY28 BASE CASE IS THE NET-NEW NUMBER: "another ~$40BN OF INCREMENTAL CAPITAL WOULD BE REQUIRED IN FY28, based on forecasted GROSS CAPEX OF ~$120BN and a similar level of CUSTOMER PREPAYMENTS as this year ($20-$25BN), WHICH WE'D ARGUE IS PROBABLY TOWARD THE HIGH END of possible capital needs."** ⚠️ **AND THE BEAR CASE IS RUN AS A SENSITIVITY RATHER THAN ASSERTED: DB flexes EPS "to assume A BEAR CASE SCENARIO WHERE 100% OF OUR ~$80BN IN INCREMENTAL FINANCING FOR FY27-28 COMES FROM EQUITY vs our base case model that assumes all incremental capital post ATM IS DEBT." So the disclosed spread is ~$80bn of FY27-28 financing, all-debt versus all-equity — that is the dilution band the market is pricing.** **DB's own view on the underlying business is unchanged and explicit: "it's understandable why investors want greater clarity here BEFORE DIGGING DEEPER INTO DEBATE ON THE BUSINESS FUNDAMENTALS AND LONG-TERM OUTLOOK, WHICH WE CONTEND IS QUITE POSITIVE."** **★ DEEPENED FROM THE FULL NOTE — the mechanics of the sensitivity, which matter because they BOUND the dilution the page keeps referring to loosely: DB flexes at "an AVG. EQUITY ISSUANCE PRICE OF $150/SH AND $100/SH" (two separate share-price grids), and derives each implied share price by taking "the FY30 EPS based on the assumption on financing mix TIMES the corresponding multiple and then DISCOUNT[ING] IT BACK TO PRESENT AT A 12% COST OF EQUITY." The published exhibits are FY30 EPS % impact vs funding mix, share-price sensitivity at $150/sh and at $100/sh issuance, implied current multiple by FY30 EPS outcome, and calendarised P/E by scenario.** ➜ **The conclusion DB draws from that grid is the actual call: "if you believe in the fundamentals of the business, long-term targets and ability to execute (all fair areas for discussion), ADDITIONAL DOWNSIDE RISK TO SHARES FROM FINANCING MIX SEEMS QUITE LIMITED." i.e. DB is arguing the market is over-discounting the funding *mix* while the funding *need* is real — a different claim from "the funding is fine."** ⚠️ **PT context the page did not carry: the $300 has stood since 2026-03-08, when Zelnick CUT it from $375 (set 2025-10-17, close $291.31); the ladder before that was $150 (Mar-24) → $165 → $180 → $200 → $240 (Jun-25) → $335 (Sep-25) → $375. Against the note's $144.39 reference and a 52-week range of $324.59-$114.99, the $300 target is ~2.1x spot.** (Deutsche Bank · Brad Zelnick / Bhavin Shah / Daniel Knauff / Yash Kejriwal, 2026-08-07) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Deutsche_Bank_ORCL_Oracle_Flexing_Funding_Mix.html) |
| **08-05** | **🔴 UBS · Karl Keirstead / Radi Sultan — "Key OCI Debates, Where We Stand" · BUY maintained, 12m PT **CUT to $245 from $285** (px 05-Aug $144.28). The most systematic bear-by-bear walkthrough on this page — it clears the New Mexico item with a channel check *and cuts the target anyway*.** | **capital / valuation** | **bull (rating) / mixed (multiple)** | **★ Keirstead takes the five concerns that have taken the stock down and marks each one. Framing: "Oracle shares are −27% POST ITS 4Q/MAY PRINT, with investors SHRUGGING-OFF strong revs growth results and guidance and focusing on several perceived risks — RISING AI INFRA CAPEX, UNCERTAIN/UNINSPIRING ROIC, EVIDENCE OF TIGHTNESS IN THE CREDIT MARKETS for new large AI data center debt offerings, DELAYS AT A KEY SITE UNDER CONSTRUCTION IN NEW MEXICO, and HIGH CUSTOMER CONCENTRATION WITH [[OPENAI]]… Net, we conclude that MATERIAL RISK IS ALREADY PRICED INTO THE STOCK and that SOME RISKS (SUCH AS NEW MEXICO DELAYS) COULD BE OVER-STATED."** 🔴 **THE NET-NEW EVIDENCE, and it is a primary channel check on the single most specific bear: "This week we spoke with an industry check with GOOD VISIBILITY INTO ORACLE'S PROJECT JUPITER CONSTRUCTION PROJECT IN NEW MEXICO. In contrast to investor concerns (AND OURS) that THE GOVERNMENT COULD BLOCK THE PROJECT, this check attached a 90% CHANCE OF THE PROJECT PROCEEDING (some delay, some higher cost) and noted that THE FIRST 200 MW IS POWERED OFF THE GRID AND IS SECURE."** ⚠️ **A named, quantified probability on a binary regulatory risk, from a check rather than an inference — and note Keirstead discloses that UBS shared the concern before the check ("and ours"), which makes the update a genuine change of mind rather than advocacy.** **On credit, the mechanism is placed rather than dismissed: "Investors are pricing-in MORE CREDIT RISK (Oracle will likely NEED TO ISSUE MORE DEBT IN CY27), evidenced in WIDER SPREADS ON PEER GROUP DEALS AND WIDER CDS SPREADS, but the HYPERSCALER PRINTS FROM LAST WEEK EASED CONCERNS and, AS KKR SAID, THERE IS SOME 'INDIGESTION' BUT THE CREDIT MARKETS REMAIN OPEN."** **On guidance: "Investors reacted poorly to Oracle's decision to effectively REAFFIRM (NOT RAISE) the FY27 revs/EPS guide on the last call, but we wonder if THERE IS UPSIDE given the material AI capacity coming online, coupled with CFO CONSERVATISM last quarter."** ⚠️ **THE THREE UNRESOLVED CONCERNS ARE STATED PLAINLY AND SHOULD BE CARRIED AS THE LIVE BEAR CASE: "further multiple expansion needs to come from an improved narrative about: 1. ORACLE'S HIGH OPENAI EXPOSURE AND HOW A CHANGING AI MODEL MARKET COULD IMPACT OPENAI'S SPEND WITH ORACLE, IF AT ALL, 2. GREATER COMFORT WITH THE ROIC (FULLY-LOADED WITH DEPRECIATION) ON AI CAPEX, PARTICULARLY ORACLE'S ABILITY TO 'MOVE UP THE STACK' FROM OFFERING CORE GPU ACCESS, 3. COMPETITION, AS [[SPCX]], [[META]] AND EVEN OPENAI ITSELF (VIA THE PIKE COUNTY SITE) SELL/CONSUME AI COMPUTE."** **Valuation: "Oracle shares trade at 11x CY28e AND 16x CY27e NON-GAAP EPS, in our view attractive given the unprecedented growth acceleration and AI exposure… At a MID-TEENS CY27 EPS MULTIPLE for accelerating growth, coupled with ALREADY-CAUTIOUS SENTIMENT, the set-up for Oracle shares seems FAVORABLE HERE."** ✅ **Corroborating desk view the same day — BofA's Brian Fenske: "the key thematic overhangs have BEGUN TO REVERSE here/now… Neocloud demand CONFIRMED through Big Tech earnings… Hyperscaler ROI getting further confirmed… IG BONDS WIDENING BEING VIEWED AS MORE SUPPLY DIGESTION RATHER THAN BALANCE-SHEET CONCERNS (with spreads now TIGHTENING)… OpenAI sentiment improvement on ARR headlines" (BofA US Insight Daily via Nicholas Villani, 2026-08-05). ⚠️ Against both: MS's fixed-income team published a podcast on "financing AI buildout / Hyperscale CDS + [[NVDA]] & [[AVGO]] backstops + ORCL" (MS Tech Talk, 08-06) — the credit channel is now a standing research topic, not a passing scare.** **★★ DEEPENED FROM THE FULL NOTE — the page previously carried this note without its price target, its estimates or its arithmetic. All of the below is net-new (UBS · Keirstead/Sultan, 2026-08-05):** 🔴 **THE PT CUT: "we remain Buy-rated, but in light of the lingering concerns addressed in this report, we're LOWERING OUR PT FROM $285 TO $245. This is based on a TARGET MULTIPLE OF 19x C28e NON-GAAP EPS (FROM 28x ON C27e), a premium to the peer group, reasonable for 30%+ revs growth."** ⚠️ **Read the construction, not the headline: the estimates did not come down (UBS EPS 05/27E $8.14 vs cons $8.06; 05/28E $10.82 vs cons $11.02) — the cut is a MULTIPLE DE-RATE (28x→19x) partly offset by rolling the valuation year forward (C27e→C28e). Same mechanism as JPM's $210 Dec-26 → $200 Dec-27 eight days later: two Buy/OW houses both cut the multiple and both hid it behind a later date.** ★ **UBS'S OWN CAPEX LADDER, and it is the largest published disagreement with the Street on this page: FY25 $21.2bn → FY26 $55.7bn → FY27 $96.6bn → FY28 $109.7bn → FY29 $74.8bn → FY30 $62.8bn. "We are HIGHER FOR FY28 ($110 billion compared to Street consensus of $92 billion) but, in our judgement, likely closer to where investors already are. For FY29, in light of the 'peak capex' commentary… we are now COMFORTABLY BELOW THE STREET ($75 billion compared to the sell-side consensus of $88 billion)."** ➜ **i.e. UBS front-loads and then rolls off — the mechanical expression of management's peak-capex claim, and the reason UBS can be more bearish than consensus on FY28 and more bullish on FY29 at the same time.** **Base facts UBS anchors on: FY26 capex $56bn, +162%, "landing above the $50 billion guidance set back in Dec 2025, and well above the previous $35 billion guidance"; F4Q capex $16.5bn, +82% y/y, FCF negative for the FIFTH STRAIGHT QUARTER; FY27 guide $90-95bn vs "~$85b buy-side consensus," ~+70% growth "BELOW our estimated IaaS/cloud infra revs growth of ~120% in FY27."** 🔴 **THE FUNDING VERDICT IS MORE BENIGN THAN THIS PAGE'S BEAR — AND IT IS CONDITIONAL: over FY27-FY30 UBS models OCF of $308bn against reported capex of $344bn, "a gap of ~$36 billion… over $45 billion of negative FCF in each of the next 2 years but then TURNS POSITIVE IN FY29." Then the conditional: "Oracle's cash capex is lower than reported capex by $20-25 billion in FY27, such that IF ORACLE CONTINUES TO NEGOTIATE CONTRACTS WITH BYOH AND CASH PRE-PAY PROVISIONS, then it is HIGHLY LIKELY THAT THERE WILL BE NO CASH FUNDING GAP OVER THE FULL FY27-FY30 PERIOD… while there may not be a funding gap over the 4-year FY27-FY30 period, THERE MOST CERTAINLY IS A FUNDING GAP FOR FY27 AND FY28."** ➜ **Triangulate with the other two funding notes this week: DB brackets FY27-28 at ~$80bn all-in (08-07), JPM at ~$20bn/yr average with $40bn in FY27 (08-13), UBS says the FY27-28 gap is real but self-liquidating by FY30 if prepay/BYOH holds. THE THREE HOUSES AGREE ON THE WINDOW (FY27-28) AND DISAGREE ONLY ON THE SIZE — which makes BYOH/prepay mix the single most load-bearing assumption on this page.** 🔴 **THE ROIC ARITHMETIC — the most damaging number in the note, and it is UBS's own bridge rather than a bear's assertion. Per GW: revenue $10bn × 35% GM → 33% op margin → $3.3bn op income → 20% tax → $2.6bn NOPAT; ADD BACK D&A of $4.2bn (capex ÷ 6-year useful life) = $6.8bn over $25bn capex/GW = 27%, "matching ORCL's 'high 20%' ROIC." FULLY LOADED — after-tax operating income ÷ capex, i.e. NOT adding depreciation back — $2.6bn / $25bn = 10%.** ⚠️ **So management's "high 20s" and UBS's "low-teens fully-burdened" are the same project seen with and without the depreciation add-back. "Most investors are not particularly excited about a 30-35% GM profile and a high-20% cash ROIC (low-teens fully-burdened by depreciation), explaining the weakness in Oracle shares." This is the quantified version of unresolved concern #2 above and should be carried as the reference calculation whenever mgmt's ROIC figure is quoted.** ★★ **AND UBS PUTS THE PRICING BEAR INTO ORACLE'S OWN UNITS — independently reaching the same gap Jefferies documented on 08-10 and SemiAnalysis asserted on 07-02: "While Oracle has disclosed a loose estimate of $10 BILLION OF REVS PER GW of capacity, investors are aware that [[SPCX]] on its earnings call this week referenced signing deals at $30-50 BILLION OF REVS PER GW. There are reasons for this gap, but there may be a STRONG UPWARD BIAS to the revs per GW assumption used by Oracle investors… the ROIC will ramp as Oracle moves well beyond 'selling GPU access' on OCI."** ➜ **Same fact as the 07-02/08-10 bear, OPPOSITE SIGN: SemiAnalysis and Jefferies read the $10bn-vs-$30-50bn gap as a permanent structural handicap imposed by contract duration; UBS reads it as unpriced upside to the revs-per-GW assumption. Both are now on the page and the disagreement is left open — it is arguably the single highest-value debate on this name.** ⚠️ **The counterweight UBS itself supplies, from "a major AI data center developer and operator": "we don't do bare metal leasing, RETURNS THERE ARE TOUGH. If hyperscalers want to own their own compute, it's going to be hard to renew a lease where you are selling bare metal. The business I would not want to be in is leasing GPUs to [[META]]. The downward pressure on the renewal rate will be VERY ACUTE. We have been told by the hyperscalers that GPU AS A SERVICE IS NOT SOMETHING THEY EVER WANT TO DO."** **TWO INDEPENDENT ROUTES TO ~$345-350BN OF CAPEX THROUGH FY30 (a useful cross-check the page lacked): (1) revenue conversion — IaaS $18.1bn FY26 → UBSe FY30 IaaS $157.3bn (note: BELOW the company's own $166bn OCI guide and far below JPM's $180bn), $139bn of dollar growth at "$2.5 of capex per dollar of future revs" ⇒ ~$345bn; (2) capacity conversion — Oracle "through our partners… secured 10+ GW of power and data center capacity coming online over the next three years," against a Crusoe-sourced modern-AI-DC build of $57.2m/MW ($19.2m construction: labour $4.7m, materials $3.9m, gas plant $3.0m, tenant fitout $2.6m, electrical $1.8m, mechanical $1.7m, soft costs $1.5m; + $38.0m IT: GPUs $30m, networking $4m, CPU/storage $3m, labour/shipping $1m) — but because Oracle leases "warm shells" from Crusoe/Vantage, "the capex to Oracle might be closer to $35 BILLION [per GW]. Across 10 GW, that amounts to $350 BILLION."** **LEASES RE-EXPRESSED AS AN EXPENSE RATHER THAN A LIABILITY — the most useful single conversion on the off-BS thread this page has been running since 06-22: "we estimate that these data center leases have an AVERAGE DURATION OF 15+ YEARS, such that $260 BILLION OF OBLIGATIONS EQUATES TO ~$17 BILLION OF ANNUAL LEASE EXPENSES (we believe that much of this runs through COGS, with cash lease payments beginning once the data centers are delivered to Oracle)." Two reads: equipment capex ramps ~a year after the lease is signed, "which explains the material jump in capex in the coming 1Q/Aug and 2Q/Nov quarters"; and "it is ENCOURAGING that Oracle's lease commits have BEGUN TO LEVEL-OFF post the big 2Q/Nov 2025 jump, perhaps a signal that Oracle is focused on EXECUTING ON ALREADY PLANNED BUILDS rather than taking on material new construction projects."** **MARGIN MECHANICS, computed rather than guided: incremental Cloud Services & Support gross margin "~39% IN FY26 (but higher at 51% IN 4Q/MAY), DOWN FROM ~55-60% IN PREVIOUS YEARS and well below the corporate GM level of 66%." On component inflation, UBS marks the risk as fading: Oracle "only does fixed price contracts when it has certainty around the cost inputs," otherwise "we do not do fixed price contracts with our customers and we have a mechanism whereby those costs end up being FLOATING" — with [[MSFT]] telling UBS the same thing post-print (fixed price BY TRANCHE of capacity for frontier labs; adjustable discounts for enterprises; long-term agreements giving line of sight on component costs). "Net, we expect this perceived risk to fade in the investor discourse over the coming quarters."** 🔴 **OPENAI CONCENTRATION, SIZED — AND THE TWO HOUSES DISAGREE: "if OpenAI's RPO balance is ~$250 billion, then OpenAI alone would represent ~40% OF THE TOTAL BACKLOG" (UBS, 08-05) versus "we estimate [[OPENAI]] represents ~50%+ of Oracle's $638bn RPO, directly and via the Stargate project" (JPM, 08-13). Neither is disclosed by Oracle. UBS: the stock is "often traded as if Oracle is an 'OPENAI PROXY'." The presumed "BIG 5" OCI customers per an industry check: OpenAI, [[MSFT]], [[META]], xAI/[[SPCX]] and [[NVDA]] ("we wonder if [[UBER]] belongs on that short list") — with a specific renewal risk that both MSFT and SPCX down-size as they stand up their own 1P capacity, and Oracle's re-rating path running through "large-scale OCI wins with TRADITIONAL NON-TECH ENTERPRISE customers."** **PROJECT JUPITER — the operational detail behind the 90%, which the page carried only as a headline probability: site designed for up to 2.5 GW with 1.2 GW FIRMLY COMMITTED in Doña Ana County NM; developers/builders STACK INFRASTRUCTURE, CLAYCO and KIEWIT, Oracle is the OPERATOR and [[OPENAI]] the TENANT. "Building permits have been granted and construction is underway… at the foundation, footprint phase," two shifts running "but they are RUNNING BEHIND given the local opposition and hence are NOT UP TO THE TARGET of having 3,000-4,000 workers on site"; the 1.2 GW is four buildings at 300 MW each. Chronology: March 2026 the NM state land commissioner rejected the gas pipeline → April 2026 Oracle pivoted to Bloom Energy solid-oxide fuel cells (Bloom shares +22%) → mid-July the commissioner pushed back again saying emissions "would still be unacceptably high," with some legislators floating a state-wide DC moratorium. 🗓️ **A formal PUBLIC HEARING ON THE AIR-QUALITY PERMIT IS SCHEDULED FOR OCTOBER 19, 2026** (Oracle and partners have filed to move it forward) — a dated, binary catalyst. The check's own words: "there is a 90% chance that the initial 1.2 GW build gets approved and built. If anything, the push-back could scuttle the UP-SIZING of the site, but not the core 1.2 GW… I suspect the US government would get involved if that happened"; on the 1H27 go-live, "I think so… they have 200 MW of power secured off the grid… enough to light up 2 of the 4 halls," with full 1.2 GW "always going to take 2+ years, let's say to the end of 2028." Also flagged: "an RFP out there to secure 500 MW of SOLAR power, just as a back-up plan and perhaps to give the developers some leverage." Corroborating non-denials: Bloom ("our 2026 revenue guidance… is not dependent on any single project" — UBS reads this as NOT a pushback on delay) and Energy Transfer on its 08-04 call ("very confident this pipeline will be put into service… maybe delayed a little bit from where we first anticipated, but we're going to get there").** **CREDIT, WITH S&P'S DOWNGRADE TRIGGERS NAMED — net-new and testable: $130bn total debt and ~$100bn net at 4Q/May against a 43% FY26 non-GAAP operating margin and a 48% OCF margin, so "Oracle remains IG, BUT IS TEETERING ON THE EDGE." S&P cut to BBB- in mid-July on customer concentration, high capex and memory inflation, conceding they "UNDERESTIMATED THE SCALE OF THE INVESTMENTS REQUIRED to expand the AI business." **S&P's stated triggers for a further downgrade: "a) leverage remained ABOVE 4.5x, b) if it believe[s] that Oracle is NOT ON A PATH TO BE FCF POSITIVE BY FY29 or c) S&P's view of the AI strategy or overall AI industry changes negatively."** Ratings row: Moody's Baa2/Negative, S&P BBB-, Fitch BBB/stable (vs [[MSFT]] AAA and [[CRWV]] B+). Market evidence: CRWV's mid-June $1.25bn senior notes priced at a 9.625% yield / 530bps spread, "a spread that through last week had WIDENED OUT TO 825BPS"; [[AMZN]]'s July issuance was "1.6x OVERSUBSCRIBED COMPARED TO 3.4x AT THE START OF THE YEAR," which UBS's credit team reads as "a market that's getting tighter and more selective."** ⚠️ **UBS's net on credit is availability-fine / price-worse: "the credit market is STILL OPEN for AI data center financing, but SPREADS ARE WIDER AND APPETITE HAS WANED SOMEWHAT. This implies a HIGHER INTEREST EXPENSE DRAG on future Oracle debt deals, BUT NOT A CREDIT AVAILABILITY RISK."** **COMPETITION — the three trends UBS says are genuinely new: (1) [[SPCX]], *an Oracle customer*, selling excess AI compute to Anthropic and Google and disclosing "another ~$13 BILLION OF ANNUALIZED REVS FROM NEW CUSTOMERS SIGNED IN THE MONTH OF JULY ALONE"; (2) [[META]] saying it has "a lot of offers for our compute at a significant premium over what we paid for it" and is "likely to begin selling capacity directly to large external customers"; (3) [[OPENAI]] partnering with SoftBank, [[NVDA]] and others on an up-to-10 GW site in PIKE COUNTY, OHIO, "a project that as far as we can tell DOES NOT INVOLVE ORACLE OR MICROSOFT (evidence of OpenAI playing a more direct role)." UBS's offset: "What Oracle has is an ENTERPRISE EDGE — SpaceX and Meta have limited inroads to F500 enterprise buyers, while this is Oracle's stronghold."** **MARKS (UBS peer table, 8/4/2026): ORCL $143.77, **-26% YTD**, mcap $424,832m; revenue growth CY26E 26% / CY27E 39% / CY28E 44% — the fastest in the ten-name set — on P/E of 19.5x / 16.0x / 11.3x vs a group mean of 19.8x / 18.4x / 15.2x. Relative performance split around the hyperscaler prints: **ORCL -40% Jan 1→Jul 29, then +25% since Jul 29** (peer average -2% then +33%; CRWV -15%/+53%, NBIS +77%/+52%, MSFT -19%/+27%).** (UBS · Karl Keirstead / Radi Sultan / Dean Marriott, 2026-08-05; BofA · Brian Fenske / Nicholas Villani, 2026-08-05; MS Tech Talk, 2026-08-06) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_ORCL_Oracle_Corporation_Key_OCI_Debates_Where_We_Stand_Bu.html) |
| 07-27 | Deutsche Bank · Brad Zelnick ('Highlights from Meetings with CFO') | capital | mixed | **DB hosted Oracle CFO Hilary Maxson and SVP of IR Ken Bond for a series of investor meetings — the first CFO-level access on this page since the AI-infrastructure funding debate became the whole story. Buy, PT $300, closing price $114.99** (i.e. DB's target is ~2.6x the current price — the widest PT-to-spot gap on this page and worth flagging as such). **Discussions were 'heavily focused on AI infrastructure, funding strategy, project execution, capital intensity, and Oracle's path to balancing rapid growth with an INVESTMENT GRADE CREDIT RATING.'** DB's takeaway is deliberately two-sided: **management 'firmly intends for Oracle to be a market leader in AI infrastructure, leveraging a customer-focused model and its technology heritage, WHILE ALSO ACKNOWLEDGING THE FINANCIAL CONSTRAINTS IN THIS EARLY HEAVY INVESTMENT PHASE REQUIRE A DISCIPLINED APPROACH TO CAPITAL ALLOCATION AND PACING RIGHT NOW.'** That acknowledgement — pacing as an explicit management posture, tied to defending IG — is the incremental datapoint. (Deutsche Bank · Brad Zelnick, 2026-07-27) |
| 07-27 | Rothschild & Co / Redburn ('Margin Call') | margem | bear | **Retains SELL on Oracle** — the only Sell in Redburn's hyperscaler coverage (vs Neutral [[AMZN]] and Neutral [[MSFT]]) — on the argument that **'gross margins are the true test of Gen-AI economics; our work shows they sit well below the 30% investors treat as a trough. We expect this to hold.'** Framing: hyperscaler shares have drifted sideways while investors show renewed appetite to 'get back in', willing to digest another year of heavy capex **so long as revenue growth continues AND margins hold** — Redburn's call is that the margin leg fails, and Oracle is where it expresses that most aggressively. (Rothschild & Co / Redburn, note 2026-07-20, distributed 2026-07-27) |
| 07-26 | UBS · Karl Keirstead / Taylor McGinnis / Jack Fyda ("Microsoft Corp. — Update on Azure, Capex and M365") | valuation | mixed | **READ-THROUGH from the UBS MSFT pre-print note, where Oracle is the cautionary comp three times over — and one line in it contests the Redburn Sell on this page.** **The reaction function, stated plainly:** "**the Street is not rewarding the stocks of AI infra providers that signal higher capex spend and/or don't post upside to cloud revs growth and margins (see Oracle and Google)**"; and on Microsoft's set-up, "we'd counter that this was true into both the recent Google and Oracle prints and **capex raises from both were not greeted with applause**." **The de-rating is dated and attributed:** "the shares of **Microsoft, Oracle, CoreWeave and other AI infra providers have been weak since early-June**," driven by the view that open models take share from the frontier labs — with UBS explicitly grouping "the hyperscalers with large frontier lab exposure (**Microsoft, Oracle**)." Note this cause is *orthogonal* to the financing/dilution bear that dominates this page. **On funding:** "**The Google and Oracle equity transactions** have raised questions as to whether Microsoft may be forced to raise external capital" — UBS puts a low probability on MSFT doing so because its shares are ~30% off the highs, i.e. reading the ORCL/GOOG raises as price-dependent. **The counterweight, and it is a genuine one:** "**We've made the same observation about the margin performance at AWS, Oracle and others — on the surface, AI capex/revs are not landing at the abysmal margin levels that the bears feared**" (context: MSFT GM -110bps y/y on $37bn / 11% of revs of AI revenue) — a direct contest of Redburn's sub-30% Gen-AI margin thesis (07-20 SELL/$110) and of SemiAnalysis's "lowest-margin rung" framing (05-27), from a house Buy-rated on ORCL at $285. **Peer-table marks (7/24/26): ORCL $114.99, -41% YTD, $335,196m mcap; revenue growth MRQ +20.6% / CY26E +25.9% / CY27E +38.7% — the highest CY27E growth of the twelve names — on 15.4x/12.6x CY26E/CY27E non-GAAP P/E vs a peer mean of 16.1x/14.4x.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ued48088.html) |

At its other sites, OpenAI RENTS CHIPS FROM CLOUD PROVIDERS SUCH AS ORACLE AND AMAZON WEB SERVICES'** (WSJ via @firstadopter, 07-22) — i.e. Oracle's largest AI customer is moving up the stack into self-build. **(2) Tape: ORCL is on Rothschild's 12-MONTH RELATIVE LOWS list** (with [[TSLA]], APP, SNAP, [[UBER]], CHTR, PSKY). Three Oracle-tenanted third-party data-centre bonds: RDMICH (Related Digital / RD Michigan Pro Owner I, 7.50% '45, 4/24/26, $14,000mn, 974MW critical IT, Baa3/NR/BBB — IG, price 101.24, YTW 6.99%, +326) · PFORGE (Applied Digital / APLD ComputeCo 2, 6.75% '31, 3/3/26, $2,150mn, 200MW, Ba3/NR/BB-, 97.88, 7.36%, +295) · YNDRDC (Yondr / JK Land JV, 6.875% '31, 6/23/26, $715mn, 48MW, Ba3/NR/BB, 93.50, 8.67%, +426) = $16.87bn / 1,222MW** _(our sum of Barclays' Figure 7 rows, not a Barclays aggregate)_ **— ~67% of Oracle's own 2026 bond issuance, raised by other people's SPVs on Oracle's covenant. RDMICH is the second-largest data-centre bond in the whole table, behind only Meta's $27.3bn Blue Owl JV.** ➜ **This is "funding relief, not resolution" in the primary market: the channel is open, deep and IG-capable (constructive), and it is Oracle's covenant being levered off its own balance sheet (the WSJ/MS off-balance-sheet critique).** <br>⚠️ **④ THE OFFTAKE-CREDIT DIFFERENTIAL IS NOW MARKET-PRICED AND ORACLE'S LEASE IS THE EXPENSIVE ONE AMONG IG TENANTS: Oracle-offtake IG at +326 vs Meta-offtake +200 (RPLDCI, NR/A+/NR) and +223 (TXEDCI, NR/A+/AA-), and Microsoft-offtake +232 (QTSQST, Baa2) and +254 (QTSODY, Baa3/BBB-) — ~70-100bp wide of the other investment-grade tenants, directionally consistent with ORCL Baa2/BBB-/BBB vs their A-to-AAA ratings.** ⚠️ **HEAVILY CONFOUNDED — NOT a clean Oracle-vs-Meta credit spread: differing tenor (2045 vs 2048/2049), amortisation, sponsor, MW scale and agency coverage, and Barclays shows G-spread for IG vs OAS for HY. DIRECTION solid, MAGNITUDE not like-for-like.** <br>**⑤ THE CONSTRUCTIVE HALF — the market Oracle leases from is scaling hard: data-centre bonds ~$76.2bn YTD ($33.6bn HY + $42.6bn IG) vs $35.9bn in full-year 2025; outstandings ~$27.3bn (Oct-25) → $112.1bn (Aug-26 MTD); five HY (STNGRY, ELNFOR, YNDRDC, GALAXY, ZENARC, $8.9bn) and two IG (TXEDCI, QTSODY, $16.5bn) priced since the June update; structures standardised (~5NC2 partial-amortising HY vs construction-risk-mitigated fully-amortising IG); ~4.0GW of near-term HY pipeline tracked, "most developers… now repeat issuers."** ➜ **The funding-AVAILABILITY question resolves in the affirmative — at Oracle's spread, not Meta's.** <br>**⑥ TWO SHARPENINGS OF EXISTING THREADS: (a) the Nvidia backstop DATED AND SIZED — on AUGUST 10 [[NVDA]] announced a non-binding MoU with Apollo, BlackRock, Blackstone, Brookfield, Goldman Sachs and KKR to mobilise >$500bn of third-party capital, NVIDIA "may provide a residual-value support mechanism for up to 25% of an opportunity"; (b) HPC hardware is ~70% OF TOTAL AI DC CAPEX and visible chip financing is $68.6bn to date / $55.2bn in 2026 (vs $5.6bn full-year 2025), which Barclays expects to accelerate.** ⚠️ **ORACLE IS NOT A PARTY TO ANY FACILITY IN THAT TABLE (all neocloud DDTLs plus the AVGO/GOOGL JV) — logged as an ABSENCE, not a negative: Barclays flags "limited visibility to private market transactions," and Oracle's BYOH/prepay structure routes hardware financing through the CUSTOMER.** <br>⚠️⚠️ **GUARD-RAIL, EXTENDING THE MS-vs-GS ONE ALREADY ON THIS PAGE TO A THIRD PERIMETER: Barclays' ~$338bn YTD (vs $179bn FULL-YEAR 2025, +$118bn since its 6/9 first note; headline rounds to "~$340bn") is USD-ONLY = IG $224.6bn + HY $39.9bn + securitised $18.1bn + visible HPC-hardware $55.2bn. NOT comparable to MS's $445bn/$217bn ("across credit channels", 08-20) nor to GS's $194bn as of 7/25 (five names incl. Oracle, ex-SPV data centres, 07-27). THREE DEFINITIONS, NONE A CORRECTION OF ANOTHER.** **Complex-level crowding, net-new: hyperscalers = 14% of ALL USD IG corporate supply YTD (~17% incl. DC bonds), 21% of IG non-financial, Tech (incl. META/AMZN/SPCX) 37% — "a record for a single sector" if sustained; ~$248bn IG hyperscaler supply YTD across currencies, Barclays forecast ~$285bn by year-end, its investor survey $275-325bn; duration ~32% 1-6y / ~32% 7-15y / ~36% 15y+ incl. ~$88bn 30y-and-out. Barclays expects supply to KEEP GROWING: "the mix is broadening to include equity, but capex is also accelerating."** ➜ **Whatever Oracle discloses on the ATM at the Sep-10 F1Q print lands into that technical.** (Barclays, 2026-08-20) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Barclays_AI_Infrastructure_Financing_Tracking_Issuance_Across_Asset_Classes_August_2026_Update.html) |
